## Session Extract — TD-pause-001 pause system (2026-07-08)
- Verdict: COMPLETE
- Two scope decisions made with the user before writing anything (per this project's Question -> Options -> Decision -> Draft -> Approval protocol): (1) engine-native `SceneTree.paused` over a custom EventBus-driven pause flag; (2) Pause Menu scoped to Resume + Quit to Sanctuary only, dropping Settings/Objective (mirroring the existing Tutorial/Help descoping precedent) and Quit to Main Menu (no Main Menu scene or SceneManager transition exists anywhere in the codebase)
- New: `docs/architecture/adr-0014-pause-system.md` (Accepted); `src/gameplay/ui/pause_menu/pause_menu.gd` + `.tscn`; `tests/unit/pause_menu/pause_menu_test.gd` (10 tests); `pause` input action in `project.godot` (Esc + gamepad Start)
- Modified: `src/foundation/event_bus/event_bus.gd` (+`game_paused`/`game_unpaused`, informational only); `design/gdd/event-bus.md` (catalogue 70→72 events, 19→20 systems); `src/gameplay/missions/the_gap/the_gap.tscn` (PauseMenu instanced); `docs/tech-debt-register.md` (TD-pause-001 closed)
- Real bug found and fixed: `wave_manager.gd:569`'s spawn coroutine `await get_tree().create_timer(entry.pre_delay).timeout` defaulted `process_always` to `true`, so scheduled mid-wave spawns would have kept firing while the game was "paused" — silently violating the exact accessibility commitment (pause anywhere, including mid-wave) this whole feature exists to satisfy. ADR-0012 had already flagged this exact risk in its own Negative/Risk section months ago but it was never resolved until now. Fixed with `process_always: false`; user approved this as an in-scope addition beyond the originally-approved changeset.
- Test evidence: `pause_menu_test.gd` 10/10 passing, 0 orphans. Full regression via `Godot_v4.6.2-stable_win64.exe --headless -s addons/gdUnit4/bin/GdUnitCmdTool.gd --ignoreHeadlessMode` (found at `C:\Users\Shaukan\Desktop\`, not on `G:\` — worth remembering for future sessions): 609/609 executed, 0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`hero_lifecycle_test.gd` AC-13, `TD-hero-005`), 26 pre-existing orphans all confined to `event_payload_validation_test.gd` (a Resource-payload test suite untouched by this change) — 0 new failures, 0 new orphans introduced by this work
- Next recommended: no High-severity tech debt remains open. S4-11 (human playtest scheduling) is now the sole remaining blocker to closing Sprint 4 via `/gate-check`.

## Session Extract — /dev-story S4-14 (2026-07-04)
- Story: `production/epics/epic-02-core-combat/story-007-hero-skill-system.md` — Hero Skill System
- Files changed: `src/core/hero/hero.gd` (extended — `_skill_slots`, `_ultimate_id`, `_skill_cooldowns`, `_ultimate_lookup`, `_skill_cooldown_timers`, `_on_active_skill_equipped()`, `_recompute_ultimate()`, `activate_skill()`, `_start_cooldown_timer()`, `_inject_skill_data()` test seam), `tests/unit/hero/hero_skill_system_test.gd` (14 tests)
- Two real gaps found and corrected before implementation (no user response to the AskUserQuestion — proceeded with the recommended options after 60s timeout, both later confirmed sound): (1) story's sketch had a public `assign_skill()` method emitting made-up signals `hero_skill_used`/`hero_ultimate_changed`; real signals are `active_skill_equipped(slot_index, skill_id)` (Hero SUBSCRIBES, doesn't expose a public method), `ultimate_skill_changed(ultimate_id: String)`, `hero_skill_activated(skill_id: String, target_position: Vector2)`; (2) no SkillDatabase/SkillResource pipeline exists anywhere — used hardcoded fields per the story's own stated MVP fallback, with an `_inject_skill_data()` test seam mirroring `GameDataRegistry._inject()`
- Two real Godot 4.6.2 engine quirks found via actual test execution (not caught by static review): (1) declaring two back-to-back empty-literal typed-Dictionary consts (`Dictionary[StringName,float]={}`, `Dictionary[String,StringName]={}`) and assigning one into a differently-typed var — even via `.duplicate()` — threw a cross-wired type error; fixed by declaring the mutable fields directly with their own fresh empty literal, no const indirection; (2) `Array[StringName].sort()` does not reliably produce lexical ordering (unlike `String.sort()`) — fixed `_recompute_ultimate()` to convert to `PackedStringArray` first, then sort, then join
- Test result: 14/14 new tests passing; full suite 420/420, 0 errors, 0 failures — verified via headless gdUnit4 run, no regressions
- Story's stale Implementation Notes sketch and QA Test Cases corrected to match the real implementation

Worth remembering for future physics-body Node tests: never-added-to-tree Nodes with server-side RIDs need the full add/remove/free cycle, not just `.free()` alone.

Fixed by injecting the real `GSceneManager` with stub paths in tests, matching the integration test's pattern, rather than testing against an isolated local instance.

## Session Extract — /code-review S3-06 (2026-07-03)
- Reviewed: `src/gameplay/wave_manager.gd`, `src/gameplay/wave_spawn_entry.gd` — 3 parallel specialists (godot-specialist, godot-gdscript-specialist, qa-tester)
- Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED → fixed → re-verified
- **BLOCKING bug found and fixed**: `_check_wave_clear_or_start_watchdog()` called `_transition_to(INTERMISSION)` synchronously from inside the still-locked outer `_transition_to(WAVE_ACTIVE)` call stack whenever the spawn coroutine resolved with zero `await` suspensions (e.g. empty `_wave_spawn_entries`, which is the default until WaveConfig loading is built) — the nested call was silently dropped by the `_phase_locked` guard, permanently soft-locking the FSM in WAVE_ACTIVE. Fix: defer the transition (`_transition_to.call_deferred(...)`) when `_phase_locked` is still true at that call site.
- Regression test added: `wave_manager_spawn_test.gd::test_empty_wave_spawn_entries_does_not_soft_lock_wave_active` — drives through the real `_transition_to()` entry point (not a direct coroutine call) so it actually exercises the reentrant path. Also fixed AC-10 test to assert final `_phase` instead of leaving the wave-clear side effect unasserted.
- Minor fixes: removed unused `_difficulty_tier` field; corrected header comment's stale "Autoload #7" claim; logged TD-wave-001 (boot-order placeholder tracking) in `docs/tech-debt-register.md`
- Test result: 26/26 passing, 0 errors, 0 failures (verified via headless gdUnit4 run)
- Next: `/story-done production/stories/core/s3-06-wave-manager-phase1.md`

**Verdict**: CHANGES REQUIRED — 3 blocking issues.

### Blocking fixes required

1. **`monsters_died_batch` contract conflict** — `event_bus.gd:215` declares `signal monsters_died_batch(payloads: Array)` (intent: `Array[MonsterDiedEvent]`) but `monsters_died_batch.gd` defines a `MonstersDiedBatch` Resource with a lossy schema (`count`, `positions: Array[Vector2i]`, `kill_sources`). The Resource also has a type error: `positions: Array[Vector2i]` vs `MonsterDiedEvent.world_position: Vector2`. **Fix**: delete `MonstersDiedBatch` Resource; change signal to `signal monsters_died_batch(payloads: Array[MonsterDiedEvent])`; update cascade test `_BatchBus` signal and batch test to match.

2. **Signal count assertion will FAIL** — `event_bus_signals_test.gd:170` asserts `is_equal(70)` but both `event_bus.gd` and the `_LocalBus` test double have 76 signals. Fix: update assertion to `is_equal(76)`, update comment block, update ADR validation criteria count.

3. **AC-14 (`sanctuary_health_depleted`) references a non-existent signal** — no such signal in `event_bus.gd`. DEFEAT is triggered via `mission_phase_changed(..., "DEFEAT", ...)`. Fix: update story AC-14 to reference the actual DEFEAT mechanism.

## Key Architectural Decisions (architecture.md confirmed)

- **Autoload boot order**: EventBus (1) → Save & Persistence (2) → Grid (3) → Pathfinding (4)
- **Pathfinding**: AStar2D (not NavigationServer) — post-cutoff avoidance decision
- **Monster scene root**: Area2D (not CharacterBody2D) — position-based movement
- **D&S deferred dispatch**: `await get_tree().process_frame` + `is_instance_valid(self)` guard
- **EventBus cascade limit**: max 2 levels; batch at 20 monster_died/frame
- **Save**: atomic .tmp → .dat; format deferred to ADR-0003
- **templates_ready gate**: Wave Manager + Monster Manager gate on _routes_ready bool
- **Tower write restriction**: Grid write API restricted to Tower System only (convention enforced)
- **WAVE_ACTIVE block**: Tower.place_tower() returns false — no queueing
- **Dual-focus HIGH RISK**: All 3 Presentation systems must test K/M + gamepad explicitly
- **FileAccess MEDIUM RISK**: Every store_* call checks bool return (4.4+)
- **duplicate_deep() policy**: Needed for nested Resources — scope defined in ADR-0008

Shipped, self-consistent contract: `TowerSystem.place_tower(blueprint_id: StringName, top_left: Vector2i)` takes a catalog lookup key and mints its own runtime `int tower_id` (via `_tower_id_counter`), which is what's passed to `Grid.place_tower()` and emitted on the bus; `tower_placed`(5 args: tower_id, grid_position, archetype, footprint, placed_in_scout)/`tower_removed`(3 args)/`tower_destroyed`(4 args) are the real catalogued signatures; the method is `sell_tower(tower_id: int)`, not `remove_tower`. Both ADRs remain Accepted (text corrections, no behavioral/code change). `architecture-traceability.md` updated: both Pass-2 blocking conflicts now marked RESOLVED — a clean `/architecture-review` PASS should be achievable on next run. (2) Decide whether Tower Gate 2 should call MissionEconomy.can_afford() once that Autoload is implemented (it isn't yet) vs keep TowerSystem's local _player_gold cache. (3) architecture.md's "ADR Audit" section still shows pre-Pass-2 stale claims, needs a refresh pass (documentation freshness only, not blocking). (4) small cosmetic items carried from Pass 2 (BOSS overlay atlas-sharing, ADR-0005 typed-Dictionary self-declaration, ADR-0010/0006 frame-budget rule scoping).
- Report: docs/architecture/architecture-review-2026-07-13c.md
- Next: /gate-check pre-production is now unblocked on the architecture side, or write the projectile-pooling-strategy ADR, or begin implementation.

Task (same session, 2026-07-13): **ADR-0019 (Projectile Pooling Strategy) written via `/architecture-decision projectile-pooling-strategy`, Proposed.** Closes the TR-tower-007 gap flagged by `/architecture-review` Pass 3. Confirmed via shipped code read that `TowerSystem._try_attack()` currently emits `tower_attacked` immediately at fire time for ALL weapon types — no projectile flight-time delay exists in code, no `projectile*.gd` files exist anywhere; this is genuinely unimplemented (BEAM/AURA are correctly instant/absent per tower.md Rules 5a/20 and need no change — only HOMING/BALLISTIC are affected). User confirmed assumptions (problem framing, GDD linkage to tower.md Rules 17-20 + Tuning Knobs, dependencies on ADR-0002/0006/0007/0009/0013), then chose both design decisions via a 2-tab AskUserQuestion: (1) **Centralized ProjectileManager** (not per-tower pool, not a generic NodePool utility) — but architected as a child node composed within Tower System's own domain rather than a top-level scene-tree peer, since only Tower System ever fires projectiles — this sidesteps the `direct_cross_system_node_reference` forbidden pattern entirely (intra-system call, not cross-system) while still mirroring ADR-0006's MonsterManager centralized-`_physics_process` pattern. (2) **Plain Node2D, no Area2D** — re-acquisition/arrival is cache/ID-driven against Tower System's existing `_monster_cache`, not spatial-collision-driven, so Area2D's physics-server monitoring overhead and monitoring/monitorable-toggle-on-reuse complexity is unnecessary. Pool sized 192 per tower.md's own formula (`ceil(max_active_towers_homing × max_attack_speed × base_flight_time_sec × 3.0)`); pool exhaustion degrades gracefully (dropped shot, not crash); re-acquisition throttled at 8/frame per the GDD's own tuning knob, with a throttled (not denied) retry-next-frame behavior for projectiles whose re-acquisition budget is exhausted. Damage delivery stays on the existing, unmodified `tower_attacked` → D&S pipeline (ADR-0007) — this ADR only changes *when* that signal fires for pooled weapon types (delayed by flight time), never how damage resolves downstream. `godot-specialist` validation: no blocking issues — confirmed the pooling pattern, the Area2D-skip decision, and the LOW engine-knowledge-risk rating; flagged two minor implementation-completeness gaps (activate() wasn't resetting stale rotation/modulate tint carried over from a pool slot's prior use; `push_warning()` has no built-in debug/release gating, contrary to the draft's "debug builds only" comment) — both fixed in the ADR before finalizing. TD-ADR skipped (lean mode). No GDD sync issues (reused tower.md's existing tuning-knob names exactly, no renames). Registry updated: 3 new stances — `projectile_pool_state` (state ownership, tower-system), `per_projectile_physics_process` (forbidden pattern, mirrors `per_monster_physics_process`), `projectile_pooling` (api decision — Node2D pool + visible/process_mode toggle, not Area2D, not instantiate/queue_free per shot). Next: run `/architecture-review` in a fresh session to confirm ADR-0019 closes TR-tower-007 cleanly, or begin implementation on Tower System's HOMING/BALLISTIC weapon combat stories now unblocked by this ADR (once Accepted).

## Session Extract — FileAccess save/load smoke test (2026-07-13c)
- Ran a live round-trip smoke test of GSavePersistence via Godot MCP (run_project background + run_script) against the actual pinned Godot 4.6.2.stable.official.71f334935 binary, not a paper/static check.
- Registered a temporary test SaveRestorer, called save_slot(9) then load_slot(9) on an unused test slot: save_ok=true, .dat written, .tmp correctly absent after (atomic rename confirmed), load_ok=true, restored data byte-identical (nested Dict/Array/bool/float round-tripped via store_var/get_var), no spurious reset_to_defaults() fallback, zero runtime warnings/errors.
- Confirms ADR-0003/TR-save-002's flagged risk (Godot 4.4+ changed FileAccess.store_* from void to bool return) is correctly handled by the shipped save_persistence.gd code.
- Test artifact (save_slot_9.dat) deleted from the real user:// save directory after verification — no residual state in slots 0-2.
- Note: MCP run_script blocks any script containing a literal FileAccess.open/DirAccess.remove_absolute call with an elicitation prompt this client doesn't support — worked around by exercising only SavePersistence's own public API (save_slot/load_slot) plus FileAccess.file_exists() checks, and cleaning up the test file via a direct filesystem command afterward instead of in-script.
- ADR-0003 and architecture-traceability.md updated to mark this Verification Required item closed.
- Remaining open item from the review: live WorldEnvironment glow/emissive smoke test before locking VFX import presets (Godot 4.6 glow rework, hero.md/tower.md).

## Session Extract — Glow rework smoke test (2026-07-13d)
- Ran a live visual smoke test via Godot MCP (run_project background + run_script + take_screenshot) against the pinned Godot 4.6.2.stable.official.71f334935 binary to characterize the "Godot 4.6 glow rework" risk flagged in hero.md/tower.md/VERSION.md.
- Built a runtime-only test scene (WorldEnvironment + overbright ColorRect on black background), never touched project files. Iteratively tested: (1) glow disabled baseline, (2) glow enabled with strong settings (intensity 6.0, strength 3.0, threshold 0.2) — no visible bloom halo appeared even at extreme overbright values (Color(4,3,0.6)), (3) discovered Viewport.use_hdr_2d must be enabled for 2D content to exceed LDR clamping and trigger glow meaningfully, (4) enabling use_hdr_2d caused the ENTIRE scene to darken globally, not just the emissive element — confirmed this persists with tonemap_mode=Linear (not a Filmic-tonemap artifact) and confirmed it happens even with glow_enabled=false (isolated: it's use_hdr_2d itself, not glow's interaction with it).
- Cleaned up all test nodes and reset use_hdr_2d=false before stopping the project. Zero runtime errors/warnings throughout.
- Actionable conclusion (documented in hero.md, tower.md, VERSION.md, architecture-traceability.md): glow/bloom VFX (Tower Affinity aura, low-HP vignette, weapon-lock glow, beam glow, constellation border) cannot be a simple "enable WorldEnvironment.glow_enabled" toggle for this project — it requires deliberately opting into use_hdr_2d, which is a whole-viewport exposure change affecting every visual on screen, not just the intended emissive element. Status changed from "Verification Required" (unknown) to "Partially characterized" (known integration cost, not yet fully closed) — still needs a follow-up pass with the actual planned VFX assets before texture import presets are locked.
- Both open Verification Required items from Pass 4 of /architecture-review are now addressed: FileAccess (fully verified/closed) and glow rework (materially characterized, follow-up recommended before final lock).

Task (same session, 2026-07-15d follow-up): **Both propagation-debt fixes applied.** User chose "fix the 2 cheap propagation-debt items now" over discussing the archetype-bucketing gap or the hero-death/lifesteal-heal race. monster.md Rule 6's payload field list corrected 5→6 fields (added `is_penultimate_wave: bool`, matching Formula 4/wave-manager.md/event-bus.md); header status note updated with the fix. pathfinding.md Formula F2's prose corrected — no longer claims "at MVP (N=1) ties never occur" / "all assignments equal the single SPAWN cell coordinate"; now states multi-SPAWN-cell maps are ordinary MVP content (matching wave-manager.md's round-robin distribution and AC-10), with N=1 stated as just one case of the general algorithm. pathfinding.md's header status flipped Approved→Needs Revision to reflect this (was incorrectly still Approved despite this pass's finding). Both GDDs' `Last Updated` bumped to 2026-07-15. systems-index.md rows for both updated to say "Fixed same-day." **Still open at that point**: the archetype-bucketing data-model gap (tower.md Rule 8 / monster.md schema / mission-hud.md Group 3b — needs a real design decision) and the hero-death/lifesteal-heal race (damage-status.md/hero.md — needs a design decision on which system owns the guard).

Task (same session, 2026-07-15d follow-up #2): **Archetype-bucketing gap resolved.** User chose "rescope Rule 8 to bucket by tier instead" over adding a new monster-archetype data field. Investigation confirmed the bug precisely: `tower.md` Rule 8's Intermission preview mandate cited "archetype distribution" (e.g. "4 Physical, 8 Ranged") — but Physical/Ranged/Elemental/Support is Tower's own Foundation-component archetype vocabulary (`tower.md` Rule 5), not a field that exists anywhere on `MonsterResource` (which only has `tier` and a single-value `archetype_tag` supporting just `"armored"`/empty). Confirmed `GameDataRegistry.get_monster(id)` is an already-established lookup pattern (referenced in multiple existing production stories/ADR-0009), so the fix needed no new schema at all: rescoped the bucketing dimension to `tier` (STANDARD/ELITE/BOSS), resolved per-`WaveCompositionEntry.monster_id` via that existing lookup. Applied across all 3 affected GDDs: `tower.md` Rule 8 rewritten (Revision 22) — new example "12 monsters: 2 ELITE"; also closed the related stale OQ-2 (Hero already subscribes to `tower_removed`, confirmed via hero.md's Interactions table) since it was found in the same area. `wave-manager.md` Rule 11 annotated (Review #13) to clarify HUD resolves tier via the registry lookup, not a new payload field — `WaveCompositionEntry`'s schema (`monster_id`, `count`) is unchanged. `mission-hud.md` (Session #3) — added new Group 3c (Rules 11f-11h): outer-HUD-root resolves each composition entry's tier via `GameDataRegistry.get_monster().tier`, accumulates STANDARD/ELITE/BOSS running totals, displays alongside the existing Group 3b boss-lane telegraph (separate concerns — Group 3b handles `boss_spawn_cell`, Group 3c handles `composition`); 2 new Group 3c ACs added; Bidirectionality notes updated. systems-index.md rows for tower.md and mission-hud.md updated to reflect the fix. **Still open at that point**: the hero-death/lifesteal-heal race (damage-status.md/hero.md) remained the only untouched item from the 4th `/review-all-gdds` pass.

Task (same session, 2026-07-15d follow-up #3): **Hero-death/lifesteal-heal race fixed.** User chose "D&S clears `_lifesteal_pending` on `hero_died`" over a Hero-side no-op guard, on the reasoning that D&S already owns `_lifesteal_pending`'s state and this keeps the guard co-located with the data it protects rather than duplicating lifecycle-tracking on the Hero side. Traced the exact mechanism confirming the bug was real: Rule III.6-7's lifesteal registration and Rule V's hero-incoming-damage pipeline are two independently-deferred (`await get_tree().process_frame`) chains with no ordering guarantee between them within a frame — so a `monster_hp_absorbed` for an already-registered lifesteal hit can arrive after `hero_died` has already fired from an unrelated, faster-resolving hit. Fixed in `damage-status.md`: new **Rule VIII** (D&S's 5th subscription, `hero_died`) clears `_lifesteal_pending` **synchronously, with no frame delay** — deliberately the opposite of Rule VII's wave-boundary drain-then-clear, since by definition no heal arriving after `hero_died` can ever be legitimate (the target is already dead), whereas Rule VII must let in-flight killing-blow heals resolve first. Rule VI.2's existing "if not present: ignore" guard does the actual suppression — no new logic needed there. Confirmed no double-clear hazard: Wave Manager's existing DEFEAT-priority rule (`wave-manager.md` Rule 14) makes `wave_completed` and `hero_died` mutually exclusive triggers in the same frame. Cascading updates: subscription count 4→5 (Rule I.2); Interactions table gained a Hero System row; new AC-25 (contrasted explicitly against AC-23/Rule VII in its NOTE); `event-bus.md`'s `hero_died` catalogue entry gained D&S as a second subscriber. No `hero.md` change was needed — the fix is entirely D&S-owned. systems-index.md rows for damage-status.md and hero.md updated to reflect the fix.

**Net result**: all 3 items from the 4th `/review-all-gdds` pass (`gdd-cross-review-2026-07-15d.md`) are now resolved — 2 propagation-debt text fixes (monster.md Rule 6, pathfinding.md F2) plus 2 genuine design decisions (archetype-bucketing rescope, hero-death/lifesteal race). Touched this session: monster.md, pathfinding.md, tower.md (Revision 22), wave-manager.md (Review #13), mission-hud.md (Session #3), damage-status.md, event-bus.md. Next: a 5th `/review-all-gdds` pass would be the natural re-verification step to confirm all of this session's fixes landed cleanly — diminishing returns are likely at this point (blocker count per pass: 7 → 6 → 6 → 3 → 0 remaining), but not yet proven zero.

## Session Extract — /review-all-gdds 2026-07-15d (5th pass, same day)
- Verdict: FAIL → all findings resolved same-day
- GDDs reviewed: 15, same set as prior passes this session
- Context: verification pass for the 4th pass's 5 fixes (monster.md Rule 6, pathfinding.md F2, archetype→tier rescope, OQ-2 closure, hero-death race). 3 parallel specialist agents.
- **Genuine self-correction, not propagation debt**: the 4th pass's own pathfinding.md fix (Formula F2, "multi-SPAWN maps are ordinary MVP content") was found to be backwards. `pathfinding.md`'s `Tuning Knobs → Mandatory constraints` section contains a pre-existing, deliberate CI gate (`AC-13d`) blocking any 2+-SPAWN mission map from shipping — the nearest-SPAWN Chebyshev algorithm is documented as incorrect near walls between SPAWNs, with a reverse-Dijkstra replacement deferred to post-MVP. `wave-manager.md`'s AC-10 (3-spawn-cell test fixture) was the actually-wrong document — it conflated "Wave Manager's round-robin code is correct for arbitrary N" (true, legitimate unit test) with "multi-SPAWN content is MVP-shippable" (false). **User chose "multi-SPAWN is post-MVP; fix wave-manager.md" over lifting the CI gate.** Fixed: pathfinding.md's Formula F2 prose reverted to single-SPAWN-at-MVP (with a clearer explanation and explicit cross-reference to AC-13d this time); wave-manager.md's AC-10 given a scope note distinguishing code-correctness from content-shippability; grid-system.md's EC-8 given an informational cross-reference note (no contract change — Grid's own API is genuinely spawn-count-agnostic, only Pathfinding's current implementation restricts N=1). pathfinding.md's header and systems-index.md's row both document the full fix→revert→correct arc for future readers.
- **Second genuine new finding**: mission-hud.md's new Group 3c (added this same session, didn't exist when the 4th-pass report was written) silently reinterpreted tower.md Rule 8's explicit "full composition... complete information" mandate for the SCOUT Wave 1 preview, rendering the same tier-bucketed view at both SCOUT and INTERMISSION instead of the asymmetry Rule 8's own Player Fantasy rationale depends on. **User chose "implement tower.md's actual intent" over revising tower.md to match the narrower implementation.** Fixed: Group 3c split into Rule 11f (INTERMISSION, tier-bucketed, unchanged) and Rule 11g (SCOUT, full per-`monster_id` list via `GameDataRegistry.get_monster().display_name` — no new schema needed, `composition` already carries `monster_id`); Rule 11h/11i renumbered; 1 new AC added.
- Also verified: monster.md Rule 6 fix holds; tower.md OQ-2 closure holds; tower.md's tier-bucketing rescope itself is mechanically sound (flagged non-blocking: it narrows the preview's information relative to the game's actual tactical axes — `archetype_tag="armored"` mitigation and `base_speed` variance are both invisible in a tier-only bucket — worth a look before Vertical Slice, not re-opened this pass).
- **Hero-death/lifesteal race (Rule VIII) re-verified at the engine-loop level, not just doc-level**: confirmed structurally sound — Monster's absorbed-flush is physics-tied, D&S's dispatch defer is idle-tied, and Godot's frame loop guarantees physics-before-idle ordering, so no interleaving exists where a pending heal can slip past Rule VIII's synchronous clear. Two **non-blocking** items surfaced, not yet acted on: (1) this correctness guarantee is emergent from an undocumented engine-timing dependency — no GDD states why Rule VIII's ordering holds, so a future refactor (e.g. Monster's flush moving off `_physics_process`, or D&S switching to `call_deferred`) could silently break it; (2) no downstream defense-in-depth exists — Hero System's `hero_healed` handler and Mission HUD both apply/display unconditionally, relying entirely on D&S never emitting a stale event. Recommended fix: a one-line note on the timing dependency plus a cheap `if hero.state == DEAD: return` guard in Hero's handler.
- systems-index.md rows updated for pathfinding.md, wave-manager.md, mission-hud.md (fix→revert→correct arcs documented, not just final state) — grid-system.md's Approved status left unchanged (informational note only, no contract change).
- Report: design/gdd/gdd-cross-review-2026-07-15d.md (Addendum section appended, not a new file — same calendar day).
- Recommended next: decide whether to act on the 2 non-blocking scenario items (Rule VIII timing-dependency documentation + downstream guard), or run a 6th `/review-all-gdds` pass to confirm this pass's 2 fixes landed cleanly, or stop the review chain here — 2 consecutive passes now (4th, 5th) have found real defects but at a shrinking rate, and one of the 4th pass's own "fixes" required a same-day correction, suggesting the value of continued re-verification is still positive but the marginal fix quality needs care, not just speed.

Task (same session, 2026-07-15d follow-up #4): **Both non-blocking scenario items applied.** User chose "apply both cheap fixes now" over running a 6th pass or stopping. `damage-status.md` gained Rule VIII.6, documenting the exact engine-timing dependency Rule VIII's correctness relies on (Monster's `_physics_process`-pinned absorbed-flush vs. D&S's idle-tied `await get_tree().process_frame` defer, and Godot's physics-before-idle frame-loop guarantee) — explicitly flagged as re-verify-required if either mechanism's implementation ever changes. `hero.md` gained a defensive `state == DEAD` guard on `hero_healed` receipt (Formula 6) plus new AC-15 (regression guard, should never fail under normal D&S behavior, exists to catch a future break in Rule VIII). Both headers and systems-index.md rows updated to reflect the fix chain in full (not just final state — fix→revert→correct arcs preserved for pathfinding.md/wave-manager.md, and the two-pass Rule VIII hardening preserved for damage-status.md/hero.md).

**Net result — full 4th+5th `/review-all-gdds` pass chain closed out**: every blocking and non-blocking item from both passes is now resolved. Touched across the two passes: monster.md, pathfinding.md, tower.md (Revision 22), wave-manager.md (Review #14), mission-hud.md (Session #4), damage-status.md (Rule VIII + VIII.6), hero.md (defensive guard + AC-15), grid-system.md (informational note only), event-bus.md. One correction-of-a-correction occurred (pathfinding.md's multi-SPAWN fix was reverted same-day after being found backwards) — a reminder to read a document's full Tuning Knobs/Mandatory-constraints section, not just the specific paragraph a finding points at, before editing. - `src/core/inventory/inventory.gd` — `Inventory` (plain scene-persistent `Node`, NOT a new Autoload — matches the control manifest's Autoload boot-order list having no Inventory slot; implements `SaveRestorer` at `restore_order=10`)
- `tests/unit/inventory/slot_stack_model_test.gd` — 16 GdUnit4 test functions covering AC-1/2/3/4/18 plus ADR-0016's own Validation Criteria (save-data primitives-only, `_next_instance_id` round-trip, malformed-data rejection)

**Deviations from ADR (both judged reasonable, flagged not hidden)**: (1) `add_item()` split into `add_unique_item()`/`add_stackable()` — the ADR's Key Interfaces didn't specify a method signature, only field shapes; splitting makes the two structurally different behaviors (never-merge vs. fill-then-overflow) explicit. Worth tracking explicitly rather than assuming it happens incidentally.

**Housekeeping**: `reports/` (20 accumulated gdUnit4 headless-run report directories, pre-existing across many past sessions, never gitignored) added to `.gitignore` — could not delete the existing untracked directories (permission denied), left for the user to clean up manually if desired.

## Session Extract — Stop hook fix + /story-readiness + /dev-story + /code-review + /story-done 2026-07-15 (Story 005)

**Stop hook fix (before returning to story work)**: user reported "Stop hook error: ... bash: .claude/hooks/session-stop.sh: No such file or directory." Root cause: every hook command (and the statusline command) in `.claude/settings.json` used a bare relative path, which only resolves if the invoking shell's cwd happens to be the project root — apparently true for `SessionStart` but not for `Stop`. Fixed all 12 hook commands + the statusline command to use `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` (Claude Code's project-root env var, confirmed via the `hook-development` skill as the correct fix for exactly this class of bug — `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is the plugin-scoped equivalent, not applicable here since this is the project's own `.claude/settings.json`, not a plugin). Verified: JSON still valid, and the fixed pattern resolves correctly even when simulated from an unrelated cwd (`CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR=... bash -c 'cd /tmp && bash "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/..."'`). **Not yet active** — hooks load at session start and can't hot-reload; takes effect on the user's next Claude Code restart, left as-is per user instruction ("leave it for the next restart").

**Story 005 (Mid-Mission Pickup Rejection)**: `/story-readiness` surfaced a real, non-obvious ambiguity beyond manifest staleness: AC-15 says "`item_added` does NOT fire" on rejection, but `item_added` had never been wired into `add_unique_item()`/`add_stackable()` at all (Stories 001-004 deliberately emitted nothing; Story 006 only wired `item_removed`) — meaning that half of the AC would be trivially/vacuously true regardless of fullness, testing nothing. Confirmed and followed this project's actual Integration-test convention (`add_child()`-based, exercising real `_ready()`/EventBus signals, per `monster_manager_wave_completed_wiring_test.gd`'s established pattern) — different from every Logic-story test used so far. Approved as designed.

Agent proposed: `Control` root (not `CanvasLayer`, correctly reasoned — no pause-while-open requirement unlike `pause_menu.gd`'s precedent), self-referencing `focus_neighbor_*` NodePath sentinel at grid edges (explicit "no movement past this edge," avoiding automatic-traversal fallback), and an `@export var inventory` with a self-instancing fallback `Inventory` child — the last one specifically enabling standalone MCP verification, which the orchestrator confirmed was actually necessary, not just nice-to-have. Approved as designed.

**Live verification phase (Godot MCP, not just code review)**: First run hit `TD-workflow-001` (fresh `class_name` not picked up by bare headless boot) — fixed via the documented one-time `godot --headless --editor --quit` rescan. Second run surfaced a genuine, undiscovered-by-static-review bug: `_create_slot_button()` connected the same `_on_input_mode_changed` method to the shared `InputModeTracker.input_mode_changed` signal once per slot (10×) — Godot's `Signal.connect()` deduplicates by `(object, method)`, ignoring differing `.bind()` args, so only Slot0's connection actually succeeded; Slots 1-9 silently never connected. This would have broken AC-14-focus's core scenario (ring must hide on mouse-mode switch without focus moving) for 9 of 10 slots — confirmed via `get_debug_output`'s "already connected" errors, not guessed at. Sent back to the implementing agent for a real fix (single `_ready()`-time connection fanning out internally via parallel arrays), not deferred as tech debt — this was a correctness bug, not a style question.

**Re-verification after the fix**: ran a precise, scripted sequence via `run_script` (not just visual screenshots) reading exact `has_focus()`/`FocusRing.visible`/`HoverHighlight.visible`/`InputModeTracker._current_mode` state through a click(mouse)→keypress(keyboard)→mouse-motion sequence, deliberately exercised against **Slot1** (not Slot0) specifically to prove the fix generalizes beyond the one slot that worked by accident before. All steps passed: mouse click sets focus without showing the ring; keyboard input moves focus via the explicit `focus_neighbor_right` wiring and the ring appears; mouse motion elsewhere switches mode and hides the ring while focus provably stays on Slot1; hover and focus confirmed as fully independent simultaneous states (Slot9 hovering while Slot1 retains focus, at the same instant). Wrote `production/qa/evidence/two-panel-presentation-evidence.md` with the full method, results table, and an explicit, non-overclaiming note that gamepad HARDWARE verification (as opposed to the code branch keyboard shares with it) remains genuinely open — no physical gamepad in this environment, MCP has no joypad-button input type.

**Files changed**: `src/ui/input_mode_tracker.gd` (new, ADR-0011 implemented near-verbatim); `src/gameplay/ui/inventory_screen/inventory_screen.gd` + `InventoryScreen.tscn` (new).

**Code review verdict: APPROVED WITH SUGGESTIONS.** Spawned both `godot-gdscript-specialist` AND `godot-specialist` (per the HIGH-engine-risk routing rule requiring an engine specialist regardless of code cleanliness). GDScript specialist: MINOR ISSUES (one style-level `get_node()` string-lookup fragility, confirmed the dedup fix pattern is sound with no other latent risk in the file). Godot specialist: MINOR ISSUES — independently confirmed the `_input()` vs `_unhandled_input()` reasoning at the dispatch-order level (not just re-stating the ADR), and surfaced two genuinely new findings beyond what live-testing already caught: no gamepad deadzone filtering on raw `InputEventJoypadMotion` (untestable in this environment), and Tab/`ui_focus_next` traversal + multi-resolution `GridContainer` layout as explicitly untested paths (not defects).

**Proactively amended ADR-0011 itself** (not just a code comment) with the signal-dedup lesson, formatted as a new "Connect `input_mode_changed` ONCE per screen" subsection in the Decision — judged worth doing immediately rather than deferring, since it directly prevents the next UI screen (Sanctuary UI, Tarot Deck UI) from independently rediscovering the exact same bug.

**Story-done: lean mode.** Both ACs COVERED via live MCP verification. **Verdict: COMPLETE.** Logged **TD-ui-001** (Medium severity — gamepad hardware verification genuinely still open, ADR-0011's own standing requirement) and **TD-ui-002** (Low severity — Tab-traversal/multi-resolution untested paths, `FocusRing` lookup fragility).

Only Resource Economy's Stories 008-015 remain in Epic 03 (independent branch, all `Ready` since 2026-07-13). 8 tech debt items now open across Inventory/UI (TD-inv-001 through TD-inv-006, TD-ui-001, TD-ui-002) — none blocking, one Medium severity (TD-ui-001, gamepad hardware).

**Story 009 (Quality Tier Roll & Menagerie Scope)**: `/story-readiness` found no real gaps beyond manifest staleness — confirmed `quality_probability_ceiling` (0.80 tuning knob) is project-wide data per the GDD's Tuning Knobs table, not per-`DropEntry` authored content, matching the established const-as-tuning-knob pattern.

Spawned `engine-programmer`, which proposed and correctly justified a "compute but don't roll/surface" scope: `_compute_quality_roll_probability()` (Formula 3) is wired into the real per-kill loop with genuine data, but no actual RNG roll or quality-tier result is performed/surfaced, since no consumer exists yet (deferred to Story 011/012) — inventing one would be premature API surface, exactly what ADR-0017's "no public call API" precedent warns against. The function's 2-argument signature deliberately excludes `menagerie_quality_bonus` entirely, making AC-04's guarantee structural rather than conventional. Approved as designed.

**Verified live**: 7/7 new tests, 33/33 combined with Stories 008/010, **766/766 full project suite**, 0 errors/failures, no regression.

## Session Extract — /story-readiness + /dev-story + /code-review + /story-done 2026-07-16 (Story 011)

User confirmed the weekly Agent limit had reset; resumed normal subagent-delegation workflow.

**Real discrepancy found and corrected before implementation began**: Story 011's own ADR Decision Summary and Implementation Notes claimed "despawns never fire either signal... suppression requires no special-case code" — directly contradicted by what was found during Story 008's code review (`monster_manager.gd` emits `monster_died` with `kill_source="wave_end"` for force-clears). Independently re-verified via direct `grep` of `monster_manager.gd` before delegating: confirmed BOSS breach (AC-06) genuinely emits the distinct `monster_breached` signal (never `monster_died` — story's premise correct for this half), but wave_end force-clears (AC-07) genuinely DO fire `monster_died` (story's premise wrong for this half — real new filtering logic needed). Corrected the story file's ADR Decision Summary and Implementation Notes in place before writing the implementation brief, rather than let the agent build against a documented-but-false premise.

Spawned `engine-programmer`, which proposed a clean 2-line guard (`if payload.kill_source == KILL_SOURCE_WAVE_END: return`) as the literal first statement in `_process_drops()`, before any table lookup — approved. **The agent then got stuck in a loop and never actually applied the guard** — it added the const and wrote the complete, correct test file, but the source fix itself was missing (confirmed via direct Read: `_process_drops()` still called `_resolve_drop_table()` unguarded). Rather than keep bouncing messages at a stuck agent, the orchestrator applied the exact approved 2-line fix directly, then ran the full test suite live via Bash (not delegated) to verify: 770/770 passing (766 previous + 4 new), 0 errors, 0 failures, 60/60 suites — confirming the fix was correct before proceeding to review.

**Files changed**: `resource_economy.gd` (const + 2-line guard + doc comments, applied directly by the orchestrator after the agent's incomplete attempt); new test suite (4 tests, fully agent-authored and correct).

**Code review**: both specialist agents ran successfully this time (weekly limit confirmed reset). GDScript specialist: CLEAN — independently re-traced guard ordering, per-payload batch independence, and cross-checked the `KILL_SOURCE_WAVE_END` const's value byte-for-byte against `monster_manager.gd`'s actual emission (including confirming no StringName/String coercion issue). QA tester: TESTABLE — confirmed AC-06's structural-absence proof and AC-07's ordering-proof (via `_unregistered_ids_seen` staying empty) are both genuine, not coincidental; one non-blocking suggestion (an all-wave_end-batch test for defense-in-depth against a batch-iteration regression).

**Story-done: lean mode.** Both ACs COVERED. **Verdict: COMPLETE.** **TD-econ-002 formally closed** (both reviewers independently confirmed the fix genuinely resolves the previously-flagged live GDD violation) — marked `[RESOLVED]` in the tech debt register with the fix method and verification recorded. Logged **TD-econ-005** (Low — the one new suggestion).

**Story 011 is now Complete.** Per the planned sequence, **Story 012 (Menagerie Attraction Material) is next** — then 014, 015, 013.

## Session Extract — /design-system defense-building 2026-07-16 (COMPLETE — pending review)
- Task: Designed Defense Building System GDD (`design/gdd/defense-building.md`) — all 8 required sections + Visual/Audio + UI Requirements + Open Questions. Authored via parallel specialist delegation (creative-director: Player Fantasy/Overview — "the foundation," the only sibling fantasy built on permanence/never-lost-on-defeat rather than a spent/staked/decaying commitment; systems-designer: Core Rules/Formulas/Edge Cases/Dependencies/Tuning Knobs, wrote directly to file; art-director: Visual/Audio + UI, wrote directly to file — established the sanctuary-overview "vacant foundation vs. built structure" visibility as the system's highest-priority requirement; qa-lead: 41 Acceptance Criteria + Open Questions, wrote directly to file).
- **Significant finding**: this GDD **delivers two exact, already-locked cross-GDD contracts** rather than just proposing new interfaces: (1) Crafting's `RecipeResource.tech_prerequisite` field is satisfied by a concrete 5-node tech tree across 2 categories (`production_tech`: tech_production_2/3/4 gating Crafting's quality tiers 2-4; `structural_tech`: tech_structural_1/2 gating structures); (2) Menagerie's Core Rule 4 "Habitat Expansion" expectation is satisfied exactly — `structure_id = habitat_expansion_tier_1/_tier_2/_tier_3`, `structure_type = "habitat_expansion"` literal on all three, Tier 1 ungated, Tiers 2/3 tech-gated, sequential-tier enforcement, cost table hand-tuned front-loaded (20/35/55 Common + 60/120/200 gold, diminishing per-tier ratios matching Inventory's own established curve-shape precedent).
- Design summary: dual currency (gold + materials, a deliberate departure from Crafting/Menagerie/Inventory's materials-only convention, justified by scale/non-repeatability), instant-on-confirm (no timers, matching Crafting's precedent), a second representative structure (Watchtower, single-tier, gameplay effect deliberately left undefined as scaffolding — not over-invented for content nothing needs yet).
- Registered `defense_building_tech_node_cost` and `defense_building_habitat_expansion_cost` formulas in `design/registry/entities.yaml`. Systems index updated: Defense Building → Designed (pending review), 2/8 Alpha systems now designed.
- Not yet run: `/design-review design/gdd/defense-building.md` — must be run in a **fresh session**.
- Next: 8 GDDs now Designed pending review this session (Constellation, Crafting, Menagerie, Tarot, Defense Building — 5 new this session, plus Inventory/Resource Economy from earlier work). Equipment Forge (Alpha, Advanced Feature layer) can now be scoped in principle — every stated dependency (Hero, Crafting, Menagerie, Constellation, Tarot, Inventory, Save & Persistence) is at least Designed — but per systems-index's own note it's deliberately designed last among Sanctuary systems since upstream revisions during review would ripple into its balance assumptions. Strongly recommend running `/design-review` on the 5 newly-designed GDDs in fresh sessions before continuing further design momentum — a real backlog of unreviewed design work has now accumulated in one session.

## Session Extract — /design-system hero-skill-build 2026-07-17 (COMPLETE — pending review)
- Task: Designed Hero Skill Build System GDD (`design/gdd/hero-skill-build.md`) — all 8 required sections + Visual/Audio + UI Requirements + Open Questions. Authored via parallel specialist delegation (creative-director: Player Fantasy/Overview — "the answer back," the first sibling fantasy where the sanctuary produces an emergent result the player didn't directly choose, contrasted explicitly against Constellation's known-formula "composition"; systems-designer: Core Rules/Formulas/Edge Cases/Dependencies/Tuning Knobs, wrote directly to file; art-director: Visual/Audio + UI, wrote directly to file — reused Tower's existing archetype iconography with zero new visual vocabulary; qa-lead: 26 Acceptance Criteria + Open Questions, wrote directly to file, correctly ignored some irrelevant injected skill-instruction content that bled into its context without being asked to act on it).
- **This is the single most design-risk-critical GDD of the session.** `game-concept.md`'s own High-Risk Systems table named this exact danger: "4 active skills × N possible combinations for the Ultimate mechanic creates a potentially explosive design space... cap Ultimate combinations at a tractable number." **Solved, not just documented**: the Ultimate is derived from the CATEGORY-COUNT PROFILE of the 4 equipped skills (categories = physical/ranged/elemental/support, reused verbatim from tower.md's existing archetype enum — zero new taxonomy), classified into one of 5 integer partitions of 4, yielding exactly 19 distinct Ultimate IDs over 35 mathematically-proven-exhaustive raw profiles (C(7,4)=35, a combinatorics identity). Critically, this matrix is provably catalogue-size-independent — growing the skill catalogue from 8 to 20+ skills never grows the 35/19 counts, since only category membership matters, not skill count per category.
- **Also closes 2 real cross-document questions**: `hero.md`'s own Open Question 3 (Ultimate lookup table authorship) and `event-bus.md`'s own Notes item 4 (passive-slot changes resolved as internal to this system, no new EventBus event — passives fold into `effect_data` before Hero reads it via the existing `hero_skill_activated` event).
- **Resolved an apparent real conflict** between `hero.md` Rule 11 ("skills are per-adventure... reset to empty on a new game") and Tower Mastery's own cross-mission-persistent skill library — concluded no actual conflict exists: slot ASSIGNMENT is freely reselectable per Sanctuary visit within a playthrough, while the underlying learned-skill LIBRARY (Tower Mastery's `unlocked_skills`) is the thing that persists cross-mission; both reset only on a brand-new save.
- **New flagged dependency**: a new Inventory `item_type = "skill_modifier"` for passive-slot items — not yet implemented on Inventory's side. 3 ACs explicitly marked [BLOCKED] pending this and 2 registry additions.
- Registered `hero_ultimate_id_derivation` (formula) and `hero_skill_category` (shared enum, cross-referencing tower.md's archetype field) in `design/registry/entities.yaml`. Systems index updated: Hero Skill Build → Designed (pending review), 5/8 Alpha systems now designed.
- Not yet run: `/design-review design/gdd/hero-skill-build.md` — must be run in a **fresh session**.
- Next: 11 GDDs now Designed pending review across this session (Constellation, Crafting, Menagerie, Tarot, Defense Building, Tower Mastery, Mission Select, Hero Skill Build — 8 new this session — plus Inventory/Resource Economy from earlier work). Remaining undesigned per the recommended design order: Equipment Forge (Alpha, deliberately last among Sanctuary systems — now has every one of its 7 stated dependencies at least Designed/Approved for the first time), Encyclopedia (Full Vision), then Presentation-layer systems (Sanctuary UI, Tarot Deck UI — both now unblocked since their dependencies are Designed). A substantial unreviewed backlog has accumulated; strongly recommend `/design-review` passes before continuing much further.

## Session Extract — /design-system equipment-forge 2026-07-17 (COMPLETE — pending review)
- Task: Designed Equipment Forge System GDD (`design/gdd/equipment-forge.md`) — all 8 required sections + Visual/Audio + UI Requirements + Open Questions, ~740 lines, the largest and most cross-cutting GDD of the session. Authored via parallel specialist delegation (creative-director: Player Fantasy/Overview — "the piece that remembers," the convergence fantasy where Constellation's composition, Tarot's wager, and Menagerie's stewardship-sacrifice all meet in one wearable object, explicitly contrasted against Crafting's "originates from undifferentiated material" fantasy; systems-designer: Core Rules/Formulas/Edge Cases/Dependencies/Tuning Knobs, wrote directly to file, ~530 lines, the single largest design pass this session; art-director: Visual/Audio + UI, wrote directly to file — the most literal "wizard's cabinet" diorama treatment yet, more so than Crafting's own panel-first screen; qa-lead: 27 Acceptance Criteria + Open Questions, wrote directly to file).
- **This is the project's designed-last, most-convergent system** — its own systems-index entry names the risk explicitly: "depends on 7 other systems being fully defined first... design last among Sanctuary systems... include an explicit 'upstream changes that invalidate this document' section." The GDD delivers exactly that: a dedicated 13-row "Upstream Assumption Fragility" table in Dependencies, each row citing a specific upstream document, the specific assumption borrowed, and the specific consequence if that document changes during its own future `/design-review` pass.
- **Closes menagerie.md's own explicitly-left-open question**: what `creature_sacrificed`'s `target_item_id` refers to — resolved as a NEW item Equipment Forge creates in the same operation, via a new `Menagerie.request_sacrifice(creature_id, target_item_id) -> bool` interface, with the sacrificed creature's species tier mapping to the new item's `quality_tier` (Formula 2, registered).
- **Two new, not-yet-implemented interfaces flagged**, both load-bearing: `Inventory.apply_rune_to_item()` (mutates an existing item's stats in-place, and — flagged by the systems-designer as "the single highest-risk fragility item in the whole document" — carves out an explicit exemption to Inventory's own Rule 5 equipped-item lock, since imbuing a currently-worn item is the common case, not an edge case) and `Menagerie.request_sacrifice()`. 10 of 27 ACs are explicitly marked blocked pending these.
- **Surfaced (did not create) a pre-existing gap**: Constellation's own GDD has never had a `restore_order` assigned anywhere in `save-persistence.md` (Constellation's own OQ-1, dated to its authoring session) — this makes Constellation-sourced rune persistence genuinely untestable today; one AC (AC-26) is explicitly marked blocked on that gap, while the equivalent Tarot-sourced AC (AC-27) is left unblocked as a deliberate contrast case since Tarot's `restore_order=22` already exists.
- Registered `equipment_forge_sacrifice_quality_tier` in `design/registry/entities.yaml`; updated `crafting_material_qty`'s `referenced_by` (Formula 1 reuses it verbatim, not forked). Systems index updated: Equipment Forge → Designed (pending review), 6/8 Alpha systems now designed.

## Session Extract — Epic-wide /code-review 2026-07-18
- Reviewed all 26 Epic 05 stories (Defense Building 9, Crafting 6, Menagerie 11) via 3 parallel specialist agents + direct review of shared files (inventory.gd, game_data_registry.gd, event_bus.gd).

  2. src/core/inventory/inventory.gd — has_items() counted locked/unique-instance slots toward availability, but consume_by_type() (the actual spender) skips them — quantity-conservation bug. Fixed: has_items() now filters identically to consume_by_type().
  3. src/core/menagerie/menagerie.gd — _tick_decay() recomputed the aggregate bonus inside the per-creature loop, so all but the last creature_happiness_changed emission per tick carried a transiently wrong value. Fixed: aggregate computed once after the full decay pass.

- Verdict: APPROVED WITH SUGGESTIONS. No remaining blocking issues.

## Session Extract — Sprint 6 close-out: S6-06b, S6-08, S6-10, smoke-check, team-qa — 2026-07-18/19
- Continued under the standing instruction "complete all of story 06, go with the recommended option and avoid prompting me" (self-select recommended/best-judgment options, no AskUserQuestion, until the smoke-check/team-qa phase where genuine human verification was required).
- **S6-06b (TD-craft-001/TD-defbuild-002 vocabulary decision) — RESOLVED**: added `ItemDefinitionResource.rarity: String` as a genuinely separate aggregation axis from `item_type`, plus `Inventory.has_items_by_rarity()`/`consume_by_rarity()` mirroring `has_items()`/`consume_by_type()`. Updated `Crafting.resolve_repair()` and (a second, newly-discovered instance of the same bug) `is_craftable()`/`resolve_craft()`/both discovery triggers via new `_has_recipe_inputs()`/`_consume_recipe_inputs()` helpers; updated `DefenseBuilding.resolve_tech_unlock()`/`resolve_structure_build()`. ADR-0021 amended.
  - **Real bug found via live MCP verification, not static review**: `has_items_by_rarity()`/`has_items()` resolve a slot's rarity/item_type via `GGameDataRegistry.get_item_definition(slot.item_id)` — NEVER from the object passed to `add_stackable()`. 5 test files (`currency_dual_gate_test.gd`, `resolution_ordering_test.gd`, `persistence_through_defeat_test.gd`, `component_repair_test.gd`, `edge_cases_shared_guard_test.gd`) were seeding Inventory with fabricated, unregistered item_ids — their "successful resolve" tests could never actually have passed. Fixed to use real registered `common_scrap`(Common)/`iron_ore`(Uncommon) content. Added `tests/unit/inventory/has_items_by_rarity_test.gd` (7 tests) and `tests/unit/crafting/mixed_vocabulary_recipe_inputs_test.gd` (4 tests, using `menagerie_lure` for a pure-item_type control to avoid a dual-axis test ambiguity). New tech-debt-register.md entry: `TD-menag-006` (unrelated, pre-existing Menagerie bug found opportunistically while running the first full-suite pass this session — not fixed, out of scope).
- **S6-08 (Constellation Stories 001-003) — IMPLEMENTED**: `src/core/constellation/constellation.gd` + `constellation_instance.gd`. Pattern catalogue (4 shapes: `triad_line`/`quad_line` via 4 direction vectors, `l_corner` via D4-symmetry transforms of one base shape generating all 8 rotations×mirrors, `cross_plus` single orientation), uniform-tag eligibility, synchronous per-event detect/activate/deactivate, reverse-index-driven overlap deactivation, Discovery Ledger as SaveRestorer (restore_order=24).
  - **Real content gap found and fixed**: `constellation_eligibility_tag` was claimed "added to event-bus.md 2026-07-17" by both the GDD and ADR-0022, but the actual `event_bus.gd` signal declarations were never updated — confirmed via grep, the field didn't exist anywhere in `src/`. Fixed: added as a trailing param to `tower_placed`/`tower_removed`/`tower_destroyed`, backed by a new `TowerData.constellation_eligibility_tag` field. This broke every existing emit/connect call site for those 3 signals (Godot requires exact arg-count match, not just "fewer params ok" as I'd assumed) — fixed `tower_system.gd`'s 3 emit sites, `pathfinding_system.gd`/`hero.gd`'s handler signatures, and ~30 call sites across 5 test files (a first-pass `perl -pi` regex bulk-edit corrupted several of those files by merging statements onto one line with tab separators — caught immediately via the next test run's parse errors, fixed manually per-file).
  - **Documented discrepancy**: `constellation.md`'s own AC-4 worked example states the `cross_plus` completion anchor as `(5,6)`, but Core Rule 1's own canonicalization rule ("lowest row, then lowest column") applied to that example's literal cells yields `(6,5)` — implemented per the stated rule, flagged in `constellation.gd`'s doc comment and the test suite, not silently "fixed" to match the possibly-wrong example.
  - Also discovered mid-testing: intrinsic pattern overlaps are real and correct (e.g. any `l_corner`'s own 3-cell run is ALSO a valid `triad_line` — Core Rule 6/AC-26 requires both to activate independently) — several of my own first-draft tests assumed false exclusivity and had to be rewritten with pattern_id-specific filtering.

- User: "start equipment forge. select the recommended options and avoid prompting me."

The 5 named slots (weapon/armor/accessory_a/accessory_b/trinket) are reserved at the LAST 5 indices (5-9) of the existing 10-slot array as permanently-locked placeholders — per `inventory.md` Rule 5a's explicit text ("locked items still occupy and count against the current slot cap"), confirming this is the textually-correct integration, not an additive 15-slot model. `equip_item()` is the sole production emitter of `equipment_slot_changed`; never calls Hero directly (AC-3, confirmed vacuously true right now since no Equipment Forge system code exists yet to potentially violate it).

Fixed all 6 to fill/check only the 5 discretionary slots (0-4) instead of all 10 — not a design deviation, the corrected expected behavior per the GDD's own pre-existing rule.
- 14 new tests (`tests/integration/equipment-forge/hero-equipment-slots-non-coupling_test.gd`), all passing. Full Inventory regression (96 tests across 8 files) clean after the 6 fixes. Project-wide full regression run in background, pending confirmation.

Menagerie-sourced runes (AC-15) excluded by construction — Equipment Forge doesn't even subscribe to `creature_sacrificed`; Story 014's sacrifice flow will consume via a direct `Menagerie.request_sacrifice()` return value per ADR-0025's own Key Interfaces, never touching this ledger. 9 new tests.

Added the 3rd discovery trigger (one-time boot sweep at restore time), completing Crafting's exact 3-trigger model that Story 009 had left at 2. 7 new tests.

## Session Extract — Equipment Forge Stories 011+012 story-done + Story 013 (Rune Imbuing Action) 2026-07-19

Hero's eventual reading of an already-imbued rune's bonus has no data source yet. Documented in code comments and this story's Completion Notes — out of AC-16's own testable scope (imbuing mechanics only), not a defect in what was built.
- Also discovered: Story 008's `equip_item()` has no path to relocate an already-crafted item into a named equipment slot (it only creates fresh instances from a definition) — "currently equipped" for a crafted item is represented via `InventorySlot.locked` directly in tests, which is what that field actually means. Not fixed (out of scope), just surfaced via the test design.

## Session Extract — Equipment Forge Story 014 (Creature Sacrifice Resolution) 2026-07-19
- Closes menagerie.md's explicitly-flagged target_item_id open question. Added `Inventory.reserve_instance_id()` (monotonic, no slot created) and an `add_unique_item()` `reserved_instance_id` param so a creature-sacrifice-born item's instance_id can be reserved BEFORE calling `Menagerie.request_sacrifice()`, matching creature_sacrificed's target_item_id exactly. New `SacrificeRecipeResource` content type (sibling to EquipmentRecipeResource) + GameDataRegistry loader/getters (`assets/data/sacrifice_recipes/`). `EquipmentForge.resolve_sacrifice()` implements Rule 19's strict order: species pre-check (AC-18) -> has_room_for (AC-20) -> reserve id -> request_sacrifice (AC-19: BREEDING_PEN rejection) -> construct item pre-imbued with 1 rune slot (Formula 2 quality tier via new static `sacrifice_quality_tier()`) -> hero_equipment_crafted then item_rune_imbued(rune_source="menagerie") in that order (AC-17).
- **Real finding, not a defect**: `Menagerie.request_sacrifice()` already existed in code — an Epic 05 story ("Story 016" per its own doc comments) landed it ahead of this story needing it, narrowing this story's actual scope to the Equipment Forge calling side only.
- **Real bug caught before shipping**: first draft used this class's own `RUNE_SLOT_COUNT_FOR_SLOT` (keyed accessory_a/accessory_b, Inventory's equip-slot vocabulary) to look up rune_slot_count for a SacrificeRecipeResource's `output_slot` (keyed accessory, EquipmentRecipeResource's vocabulary) — wrong table, would have silently produced incorrect rune slot counts for accessory-slot sacrifices. Fixed to use `EquipmentRecipeResource.RUNE_SLOT_COUNT_FOR_SLOT` instead.
- **Deliberate convention decision, flagged not silent**: hero_equipment_crafted/item_rune_imbued's item_id kept as the base definition id (Story 010/013's shipped precedent) rather than Rule 19(f)'s literal "item_id = target_item_id" wording, to keep the two signals' payload shape consistent across craft/imbue/sacrifice.
- Content authored for 2 of the eventual 5 sacrifice recipes (aurora_stag Tier-3->4, glimmer_hare Tier-1->2) — enough to exercise both Formula 2 boundary worked examples; full 5-species catalogue explicitly out of scope (content-authoring work).
- 7 new tests, all passing. Full project regression run in background, pending confirmation.
- Next: story-done for 014, then Story 015 (Concurrency Guard & Orphaned Rune References, ADR-0025) — the full 3-operation shared `_forge_resolving` guard.

Implementing full AC-24 would require expanding into Story 013's already-documented territory, so this story left it explicitly partial rather than silently narrowing its own scope to claim false completion.

- **ADR-0031 (Mission HUD Architecture)** written and Accepted (explicit user decision, outside normal /architecture-review gate, matching established session precedent). MissionHud (CanvasLayer, static Mission-scene child per already-Accepted ADR-0002) -> persistent CombatCluster + re-instantiated-per-wave-cycle PlanningCluster (own PackedScene). 5 typed Rule-4 state fields on the outer root. 3 direct-Autoload calls classified under ADR-0020 (2x Criterion A read: Pathfinding.get_path()/Grid.cell_to_world(); 1x Criterion B action: MissionSelect.retry_current_mission()). Reuses ADR-0011's InputModeTracker verbatim. No SaveRestorer — Mission HUD owns zero persisted state.
- **Real bug caught during engine-specialist review**: first draft's Planning Cluster teardown used `queue_free()` alone — but queue_free() defers node removal to end-of-frame, leaving the outgoing instance's EventBus subscriptions live and DOUBLE-CONNECTED against a freshly-instantiated replacement for the rest of that frame. Fixed: synchronous `remove_child()` first (forces immediate `_exit_tree()`), THEN `queue_free()`. Also had the specialist name a concrete cell-to-screen-space API (`get_viewport().get_canvas_transform() * Grid.cell_to_world(cell)`) rather than leaving it "TBD, verify later." Registry updated with 1 new api_decision (`mission_hud_planning_cluster_lifecycle`).

## Session Extract — Mission HUD: real regression caught by full-suite discipline 2026-07-20
- Story 002's full-project regression showed "3 errors" instead of the usual "0 errors, 1 pre-existing failure" — investigated rather than dismissed as noise. Root cause: Story 002 extended MissionHud._on_mission_phase_changed()'s SCOUT-mount branch to call _spawn_planning_cluster(), which dereferences the @onready-resolved _planning_cluster_host — but Story 001's OWN test file predated that addition and used bare MissionHud.new() with no add_child(), so that reference stayed null, crashing 3 of Story 001's own tests with "Cannot call method 'add_child' on a null value."
- This is exactly the kind of cross-story regression the "run full regression after every story, don't just trust the new story's own green tests" discipline exists to catch — Story 001's own isolated test run still passed (its 3 crashing tests never triggered the SCOUT-mount branch's cluster-spawn path in isolation... actually they did, but only surfaced when run in the SAME suite as later stories' fixture-building conventions diverged). Fixed by updating Story 001's test fixture to match Story 002/003's now-established add_child()-based convention. Re-verified: 29/29 across all 3 mission-hud test files, 0 errors.

Confirmed the Story 006 regression left pending at the prior stopping point.
- **Real, previously-undetected bug found in `tests/gdunit4_runner.gd`'s own documented CLI usage**: the doc comment's example invocation (`godot --headless --script tests/gdunit4_runner.gd -- -a res://tests --ignoreHeadlessMode`) silently runs ZERO tests and prints only the help text — no error, exit code 0. Root cause: `GdUnitTestCIRunner`'s underlying `CmdArgumentParser.parse()` (`addons/gdUnit4/src/cmd/CmdArgumentParser.gd:14-24`) pops arguments off the front of `OS.get_cmdline_args()` looking for one that **contains the substring `"GdUnitCmdTool.gd"`** as a sentinel marking "everything before this is Godot/engine args, everything after is ours" — but our wrapper script is named `gdunit4_runner.gd`, which never matches, so the sentinel-search loop silently consumes the entire args array and returns empty, triggering the help-text fallback. This is a wrapper/addon mismatch, not new in this session — every `/smoke-check` or manual regression run using the documented command has likely been running 0 tests and reporting false-clean this entire project (unconfirmed how far back — worth checking `/smoke-check`'s own invocation matches this). **Fix/workaround**: insert a dummy token containing `"GdUnitCmdTool.gd"` before the real args: `godot --headless --path . --script tests/gdunit4_runner.gd GdUnitCmdTool.gd -a res://tests --ignoreHeadlessMode -c`. Recommend updating `tests/gdunit4_runner.gd`'s own doc comment (and any CI/skill invocation of it) to this corrected form — **not done this session**, flagged here instead since it touches a shared test-infra file outside this session's Mission HUD scope.
- Also confirmed (via a throwaway `argdump.gd` diagnostic script) that Godot 4.6.2's `OS.get_cmdline_args()` does NOT include arguments after a `--` separator (that's `OS.get_cmdline_user_args()`'s job) — opposite of the behavior the addon's own naive sentinel-search was implicitly relying on. Not filed as an engine-reference finding since the actual root cause is the addon's sentinel string, not this `OS` API itself.
- Godot 4.6.2 binary located at `C:\Users\Shaukan\Desktop\Godot_v4.6.2-stable_win64.exe` (not on any drive's PATH; the only other Godot binary found on this machine was an unrelated 4.4.1 mono build in `G:\`).
- **Full regression confirmed clean with the corrected CLI invocation**: 1415/1415 test cases (141/141 suites), 0 errors, 1 pre-existing failure (`lure_acquisition_test.gd::test_lure_creature_success_full_flow`, the same tolerated `TD-menag-006` Menagerie enum bug from every prior session), 66 orphans (pre-existing, not investigated — no session before this one has treated orphan count as a gate). Confirmed Story 006's `wave_preview_available` signal-extension blast radius is genuinely safe.

- **Real API/type drift resolved before implementing** (same class of gap this epic has repeatedly hit): the story's own Implementation Notes pseudocode referenced a `WaveCompositionEntry` class and `GameDataRegistry.get_monster()` — neither exists. Real shape: `WaveManager._build_composition_preview()` returns `Array[Dictionary]` (`{"monster_id": StringName, "count": int}`); real registry method is `get_monster_resource(id) -> MonsterResource` (`tier: int` 0/1/2, not a String).   - **Real architecture gap found, flagged not silently assumed**: the GDD's own DEFEAT row states a separate "outcome/Defeat screen" takes over from Mission HUD entirely on DEFEAT (Retry lives there) — that screen does not exist anywhere in this codebase (confirmed via search — no story or epic has built it). Implemented as a pragmatic placeholder: `RetryButton` added directly to `MissionHud`'s own `CanvasLayer` in `the_gap.tscn`, with the terminal-phase branch changed so DEFEAT specifically keeps the whole CanvasLayer `visible = true` (hiding only `_combat_cluster`) so Retry can actually be seen/pressed — MISSION_COMPLETE keeps the prior fully-hidden behavior. Flagged at both the field's doc comment and the story file for replacement once a real Outcome/Defeat screen exists.
  - **Real, more serious bug found and fixed**: AC-70 requires Mission HUD to react to a retry's `mission_launched` "exactly like an initial mission launch." Traced the actual mechanics (not just documented the requirement): `WaveManager._reset_all_state()` (called from `_on_mission_launched()`, itself called by `retry_current_mission()`) never resets `WaveManager`'s own internal `_phase` field back to `IDLE` before transitioning to `SCOUT` — so a retry's `mission_phase_changed` genuinely arrives as `(previous_phase="DEFEAT", new_phase="SCOUT")`. Story 001's original mount condition (`previous_phase == "IDLE" and new_phase == "SCOUT"`) would silently NOT match this, meaning Mission HUD's entire Rule-4 reset/Planning-Cluster-respawn path would never fire on a real retry — a genuine, previously-undiscovered functional bug, not a hypothetical. Fixed by dropping the `previous_phase` condition entirely (`WaveManager`'s FSM only ever enters `SCOUT` as a mission-start transition, never mid-mission, confirmed by reading every `_transition_to(Phase.SCOUT)` call site — there's exactly one, in `_on_mission_launched()`).
  - 1 pre-existing test fixed: `hud-scaffold-local-state_test.gd`'s `test_defeat_also_unmounts` asserted `_hud.visible == false` after DEFEAT, now deliberately `true` — renamed/rewritten to assert the real new behavior (`_hud.visible == true`, `_combat_cluster.visible == false`).
  - 7 new tests in `tests/integration/mission-hud/defeat-retry-button_test.gd`, all passing (104/104 across all 9 mission-hud test files). Live-MCP-verified end-to-end against the real running `the_gap.tscn`: a full DEFEAT→retry→SCOUT cycle confirmed Retry visibility, CombatCluster visibility, Rule-4 state reset, and Planning Cluster respawn all correct; a second live check with the real `GMissionSelect` Autoload armed via `select_mission`/`set_difficulty_tier`/`confirm_launch` confirmed pressing Retry fires `mission_launched` exactly once via the direct call. That second live check also surfaced (not caused by this story) a **pre-existing, unrelated content gap**: "the_gap" has no `_waves.tres` authored yet, and `res://src/scenes/SanctuaryScene.tscn` doesn't exist — both cause real runtime errors when the full launch→MISSION_COMPLETE→Sanctuary-transition pipeline is exercised end-to-end, independent of Mission HUD. Worth a project-level note but explicitly not this epic's problem.
  - Full project regression: 1470/1470, 0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`TD-menag-006`).
- **Epic 09 (Mission HUD) is now 12/12 stories Complete, all genuinely verified.** `EPIC.md` and `production/epics/index.md` updated to `Status: Complete`. 4 known real gaps carried forward, all explicitly flagged in the epic doc: AC-62's world-position placeholder, AC-68's Retry-button pragmatic-placeholder location, the still-stale TR registry, and the still-deferred batch `/code-review` across all 12 stories (matching the Epic 08/Equipment Forge precedent — not yet run for this epic).

## Session Extract — "Keep going": investigated the "no wave content for the_gap" gap, found and fixed the REAL bug (GridConfig.cells missing @export) 2026-07-20

- **The earlier session note claiming "no `_waves.tres` exists for `the_gap`" was WRONG** — `assets/data/waves/the_gap_waves.tres` genuinely exists with real, full 6-wave content (goblin_runner/forest_troll_elite/goblin_warlord_boss). Investigated the actual error from the earlier Story 012 live-MCP session more carefully — the real logged error was `"WaveManager: 0 spawn cells"`, not "no WaveConfig found." Misattributed at the time; corrected here.
- **Root cause traced to `assets/data/missions/entries/the_gap_entry.tres`** — Mission Select's real shipped mission entry for "the_gap" — whose `grid_config` sub-resource had 0 cells (only `cols`/`rows`/`cell_size` set, no actual SPAWN/PATH/GOAL layout). This is what `MissionSelect.confirm_launch()`/`retry_current_mission()` hands to `GGrid.initialize()` in the real launch pipeline — 0 spawn cells there, not in the wave content, is what triggered the immediate `MISSION_COMPLETE` bypass.
- **Found the actual underlying bug, one layer deeper**: `GridConfig.cells` (`src/foundation/grid/grid_config.gd`) was declared as a plain `var`, missing `@export` — so `ResourceSaver.save()` silently dropped it from ANY `.tres` file, always, regardless of what was set on the in-memory instance. This directly contradicted the class's own doc comment ("Construct programmatically... or author as a .tres file") — .tres authoring was never actually possible for this field. `the_gap.tscn`'s own direct-boot debug path (`the_gap.gd`'s `_build_grid_config()`, built programmatically at runtime) was unaffected — this is exactly why the bug went unnoticed through the whole project so far: nobody had launched a mission through the *real* Mission Select pipeline in an automated test before.
- **Fixed**: added `@export` to `GridConfig.cells`. Regenerated `the_gap_entry.tres`'s `grid_config` to exactly match `the_gap.gd`'s real programmatic layout — done via a live MCP script that instantiated `TheGap` directly (safe: `_build_grid_config()` touches no Autoloads/`_ready()` state) and called its own `_build_grid_config()`, guaranteeing byte-for-byte parity rather than hand-transcribing 192 cells. Verified via a full round-trip: `ResourceLoader.load(..., CACHE_MODE_IGNORE)` (forces a genuine disk read, not the in-memory cache) confirmed 192 cells, correct SPAWN/GOAL types; then confirmed live via the real `GGrid`/`GPathfindingSystem` Autoloads that a real 29-cell path resolves; then confirmed the real `GMissionSelect.confirm_launch()` pipeline no longer bypasses to `MISSION_COMPLETE`.
- 3 new regression tests: `tests/unit/grid/grid_config_persistence_test.gd` (generic save/reload round-trip, would have caught this bug directly), `tests/integration/mission-select/the-gap-entry-content_test.gd` (2 tests — content-integrity check on the actual shipped file, plus a byte-for-byte comparison against `the_gap.gd`'s own programmatic layout so the two sources of truth can never silently drift again). All passing (88/88 in the targeted grid/mission-select suites). Full project regression: 1479/1479, 0 errors, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`TD-menag-006`).
- **`the_narrows_entry.tres` has the identical 0-cells symptom but was deliberately NOT fixed** — no real scene exists for "the_narrows" anywhere in `src/gameplay/missions/` (only `the_gap` does); it's used purely as a second `mission_id` in Mission Select's own unit tests (selection/completion-tracking scenarios that never touch grid content). Fabricating real level geometry for it would be inventing content without any design basis — flagged, not fixed.
- Updated `production/epics/epic-01-foundation.md` with a full retroactive note (this is a Foundation-layer/Epic-01 bug, even though it was discovered via Mission HUD/Epic 09 work).
- **This closes one of the 2 project-level gaps flagged in the earlier Mission HUD session extract.** The other (`res://src/scenes/SanctuaryScene.tscn` doesn't exist, so `MISSION_COMPLETE`'s scene transition still hard-errors in real play) remains open — that one is genuinely new-content/new-scene work, not a bug fix, and needs a design decision about what the Sanctuary scene should contain (ties into the still-"Not Started" Sanctuary UI system).
- **Next**: `SanctuaryScene.tscn` not existing is now the single most consequential remaining project-level gap for actual playability — recommend that as the next concrete continuation if the user wants to keep going in this vein, though it likely needs at least a minimal Sanctuary UI design decision (even a stub scene) before implementation, unlike this session's GridConfig fix which was pure bug-fixing with an unambiguous correct answer already present in the codebase.
