## Session Extract — /design-system sanctuary-ui COMPLETE 2026-07-20

- **All sections written and approved.** `design/gdd/sanctuary-ui.md` — Overview, Player Fantasy ("The Curator of Your Own Cabinet"), Detailed Design (9 Core Rules + States/Transitions + Interactions table), Formulas (None, justified), 12 Edge Cases + terminal-latch rule, Dependencies, Tuning Knobs (6 knobs), Visual/Audio Requirements, UI Requirements (defers full spec to future `/ux-design` pass, UX Flag raised), Acceptance Criteria (9 Core Rule groups + States + Cross-System + Load-Bearing Edge Cases + Experiential Criterion), Open Questions (4 items). Status: `Designed (pending /design-review)`. CD-GDD-ALIGN skipped per Lean mode.
- **The central design problem this GDD resolves**: none of the 5 wrapped systems' own GDDs (Inventory+Crafting, Menagerie, Defense Building, Equipment Forge) assumed a unifying shell existed — Inventory+Crafting is one fused screen, the other 3 are independently-shaped separate screens, and Defense Building/Menagerie both wanted a persistent always-visible overview layer with no single document reconciling any of this. This GDD originates the resolution: a persistent Sanctuary-overview diorama base layer + a left-edge tab-strip navigating 5 rooms (Overview + 4 work-rooms) + docked room panels that leave a fixed margin so the diorama stays visible always.
- **Two specialist agents (game-designer, ux-designer) genuinely disagreed** on the navigation model — presented both to the user via `AskUserQuestion` rather than picking one silently. User chose the ux-designer's model on 3 of 4 sub-decisions (persistent tab strip over overview-as-map; `LT`/`RT` triggers over `LB`/`RB` bumpers — this one was a real caught conflict, not just preference, since `LB`/`RB` is already committed to in-room tab switching in 3 of the 5 wrapped systems; always-visible base layer over full-canvas-room-plus-periphery-strip) and the ux-designer's proposed fix for Menagerie's habitat-view inconsistency (zoom-in-on-overview-slot).
- **QA-lead's review caught 2 real internal inconsistencies** in the drafted Core Rules before Acceptance Criteria could be written against them: Core Rule 6 said "six instances" but only listed 5 (4 rooms + 1 shell) — fixed by recognizing Overview's own structure-selection interaction needs its own `InputModeTracker`, making 6 genuinely correct. Core Rule 7 and the States/Transitions table described Cancel-at-room-root two different ways (focus-only change vs. full state transition) — fixed by making Core Rule 7 explicitly match the States table (full transition to `OVERVIEW`). Both fixed before the AC section was finalized against them.
- **4 Open Questions carried forward**, most notably: `menagerie.md` itself was not updated to reflect this GDD's Core Rule 4 resolution (its own habitat description still reads as a full-bleed scene) — flagged for the Game Designer to decide whether menagerie.md needs a cross-reference note.
- `systems-index.md` updated: Sanctuary UI row now `Designed (pending /design-review)`, linked to the new GDD file, dependency list expanded (added SceneManager/Mission Select/Resource Economy, previously only listed the 5 wrapped systems + Save & Persistence). Progress Tracker counts updated (22 design docs started, 7/8 Alpha systems designed).
- **Next recommended step**: run `/design-review design/gdd/sanctuary-ui.md` in a **fresh session** (never inline — the reviewing agent must be independent of this authoring context). After that, `/create-epics`/`/create-stories` can proceed once Approved, though implementation should wait for a companion `/ux-design` pass per the UX Flag raised in the GDD's own UI Requirements section. The 3 other "Not Started" systems (Tarot Deck UI, Encyclopedia, Audio) remain queued behind this one per the priority tiers already established.
- **Next session should**: (1) confirm the pending full regression result, (2) if clean, proceed directly to Story 007 (Wave Composition Preview Display) — depends only on Stories 002+006, both Complete — then continue through 008→012 in the epic's stated dependency order (008 unlocks 009/010/012).

## Session Extract — Autonomous overnight session 2026-07-24: Sanctuary UI MAJOR REVISION resolved, UX spec authored, Tarot Deck UI GDD authored from scratch

- **User instruction for this session**: "continue production on your own... always go with the recommended option if there are any decisions to make... avoid reviews" — no interactive checkpoints, autonomous end-to-end. All decisions below were made by this session (with specialist subagent consultation) rather than approved by the user in real time; flag anything for revision on first read.

### Sanctuary UI GDD: MAJOR REVISION found and fixed same-session
- Ran the queued next step from the prior session (`/design-review design/gdd/sanctuary-ui.md`, full mode: game-designer, systems-designer, ux-designer, ui-programmer, qa-lead, godot-specialist, creative-director). **Verdict: MAJOR REVISION NEEDED.** Root cause (named explicitly by creative-director's synthesis): the original structural model — diorama always visible in an 18%/10% margin + room panel docked into the remainder — was undersized on two axes at once. The margin was too thin to read (ux-designer: ~194px/~192px at 1080p); the rooms didn't fit what they wrap (ux-designer + ui-programmer, confirmed against real code that `InventoryScreen.tscn` is authored full-rect). Worse, Core Rule 9's ban on cross-room highlighting directly contradicted the Player Fantasy's own central promise (game-designer).
- **Fixed same-session**: re-architected to "diorama owns `OVERVIEW` fully, rooms get the full working frame (just tab strip + header reserved), cross-system legibility via new tab-strip **Connection Badges** instead of simultaneous panel visibility." This let Core Rule 4's entire camera-zoom mechanism be deleted outright (Menagerie's full-bleed habitat no longer conflicts with anything). Added: explicit destroy-and-recreate room lifecycle (Core Rule 6, mirrors `mission_hud.gd`'s Planning Cluster precedent) with a `set_process_unhandled_input(false)`-before-`queue_free()` rule; explicit `OVERVIEW` default-focus rule; a tightened terminal-latch (disconnect-not-just-flag); a one-time tab-strip onboarding tooltip; a non-blocking "staged changes discarded" toast; 3 new/rewritten ACs for previously-uncovered edge cases; confirmed no room-relevant `.tscn` in this project uses `CanvasLayer` (clearing a conditional godot-specialist blocker for the 1 screen that exists; deferred for the 3 that don't, OQ-5).
- **Independent re-verification**: spawned a fresh-context `creative-director` agent (no prior context, read the doc cold) specifically to check the revision's own claims. Confirmed all 6 structural defects genuinely, structurally closed — "a real re-architecture, not word-padding." Found 2 remaining documentation-only gaps (chrome z-order vs. a future CanvasLayer-using room; the Overview section's "first document to define" claim was never actually corrected in prose despite being flagged as needing it) — both fixed same-session.
- `design/gdd/reviews/sanctuary-ui-review-log.md` created with full detail. `design/gdd/systems-index.md` updated. Status header kept conservative (`Needs Revision`, not flipped to `Approved`) per this project's own precedent (`mission-hud.md` only flipped to Approved after an actual `/design-review` re-run, not a same-session self-fix) — **recommend one more formal `/design-review` pass in a genuinely fresh session before this gates story-writing.**

- Found this stalled GDD while looking for other queued work; picked it up in parallel with the Sanctuary UI UX-spec agent (different files, no conflict).

Also caught and flagged (not fixed): `hero-skill-build.md` still self-cites a stale `restore_order = 32` in its own prose; this document used the correct authoritative value (31) from `save-persistence.md`'s table instead, and flagged the source document's drift as OQ-2.

Logged to `design/gdd/reviews/tarot-deck-ui-review-log.md`. `systems-index.md` updated — **all 8 Alpha-priority systems now have a GDD for the first time.**

### Tarot Deck UI: full `/design-review` found MAJOR REVISION NEEDED — fixed and independently re-verified same session
- Ran the full adversarial pass (game-designer, systems-designer, ux-designer, ui-programmer, qa-lead, creative-director) on `design/gdd/tarot-deck-ui.md`, superseding the earlier same-day solo self-check (which had wrongly returned APPROVED). **The full pass found 5 real blocking defects the solo check completely missed**, most notably: the Player Fantasy's "juxtaposition" claim was structurally impossible under the shell's own mutual-exclusion model (the two screens can never be visible together); the one candidate bridging mechanic (a Tarot/Mission-Select conflict indicator) never connected to Hero Skill Build at all; and that indicator's own match logic compared two disjoint string vocabularies (`tarot.md`'s `modifier_type` values vs. `mission-select.md`'s `modifier_id` values) — confirmed via direct file reads — meaning it could never actually fire.
- **Fixed same-session**: added a new Core Rule 7 ("Current Build Snapshot") — a genuine, bounded, read-only cross-screen chip showing each wrapped screen's headline derived value on the other screen, introducing no new formula/event/data-ownership, only a display surface. Rewrote the Player Fantasy to ground itself in this smaller, real mechanic rather than the unearned "same frame" claim. Gave `sanctuary-ui.md` a brand-new Core Rule 10 (a dedicated "Loadout" affordance in `OVERVIEW`, same tier as Launch/Depart, with its own `DEPARTING_TO_LOADOUT` state, edge cases, and 4 ACs) to replace the broken diorama-click entry-point reuse. Replaced the broken string-equality conflict check with an explicit conceptual mapping table (`tarot_difficulty_growth_rate` ↔ `difficulty_growth_boost`; `tarot_wave_config_override` ↔ both `extend_waves` and `difficulty_growth_boost`, the second pairing added during independent re-verification). Restated the destroy-and-recreate lifecycle mechanism explicitly instead of only citing it by reference. Closed all AC/edge-case gaps qa-lead found.
- **Independent fresh-context re-verification** (a `creative-director` agent with no prior context, same rigor applied to Sanctuary UI): confirmed all 5 blocking problems structurally closed, traced the new mapping table personally against both source GDDs' actual catalogues, and found 2 further minor residual issues (stale leftover references to the old diorama-click entry mechanism; the mapping table's `tarot_wave_config_override` row only covered one of its two real conflicts) — both fixed immediately.
- **This document is now marked `Approved`** in `systems-index.md` — the only GDD this session upgraded to that status, since it received both a full adversarial pass *and* an independent re-verification pass, matching the rigor `mission-hud.md` required before its own Approved status. `design/gdd/reviews/tarot-deck-ui-review-log.md` records the full history honestly, including the solo self-check's wrong initial verdict — a lesson explicitly recorded for future sessions: narrow/small-looking scope does not mean a design review can be skipped or lightened, since one of the 5 missed defects (the namespace mismatch) was verifiable just by reading the two already-written source files.
- All 8 Alpha-priority systems now have a GDD; 2 of them (Hero Skill Build, Tarot Deck UI) are `Approved`.

### Sanctuary UI: 4th pass — targeted consistency check on the Core Rule 10 addition
- After Tarot Deck UI's review added Core Rule 10 to `sanctuary-ui.md`, spawned one more fresh-context agent (systems-designer, no prior context) specifically to check that addition's internal consistency — not a full re-review, a targeted check appropriate to the size of the change. Found and fixed 4 real gaps: the terminal-latch Edge Case only named the Launch/Depart handler, leaving `DEPARTING_TO_LOADOUT` covered by an unstated cross-reference; neither Launch/Depart nor Loadout had a stated `InputModeTracker` owner; navigating from the diorama's structure-selection ring to either button was undefined; the Dependencies table was missing a Tarot Deck UI row entirely. All 4 fixed same-session — see `design/gdd/reviews/sanctuary-ui-review-log.md`'s "Consistency Check" entry for full detail.
- `sanctuary-ui.md` has now been touched 4 times this session (original MAJOR REVISION fix → independent re-verification fixes → OQ-1 documentation fix → Core Rule 10 structural fix + this consistency check). A genuinely fresh-context `/design-review` remains the recommended gate before Approved status — see next steps below.

### Sanctuary UI: 4th pass (fully fresh full `/design-review`) found the pattern itself was the problem — document frozen, not patched further
- Ran a genuinely fresh full review (6 specialists, none with prior session context) on `sanctuary-ui.md` as it stood after 3 same-session patches. **5 of 6 specialists independently converged on the same target**: Core Rule 10 (the Loadout affordance) and its focus-ownership fix (added in the prior, 3rd pass). Real defects found: an unguarded terminal-latch race between the two exit buttons (confirmed against `mission_hud.gd`'s actual source — the cited precedent doesn't cover a two-independent-triggers race, so the document's "mirrors it exactly" claim was false); a self-contradictory tab-strip entry count (5 vs. 7, untested); an internally incoherent focus-position description ("tail entries... immediately after Overview," which are two different positions since Overview is first, not last); no mechanism excluding the 2 action buttons from focus during `ROOM_*` states despite ACs requiring it; Core Rule 10 never integrated into Player Fantasy/Overview; and the sibling UX spec both omitting Loadout entirely and actively disagreeing with the GDD on traversal order. Separately, game-designer found (independent of Core Rule 10) that Connection Badges — believed closed since the 1st pass — don't actually convey *which* choice caused a change, only that one happened.
- **creative-director named this pattern explicitly: fix-induced-defect cascade / revision fatigue.** The clearest evidence: the focus-ownership fix's own stated rationale was "keep Core Rule 6's six-trackers claim exactly accurate" — optimizing to preserve a prior numeric assertion rather than designing the affordance on its own terms. Corroborating evidence: the *original* MAJOR REVISION's own root defect (asserting screen-space fits without verifying against real content) was found to have recurred one level deeper, for the tech-graph/Imbue-mode space budget.
- **Explicit directive followed, not overridden**: creative-director recommended freezing the document — redesigning Core Rule 10 deliberately in its own dedicated pass rather than patching it a 5th time in the same continuous session, since a 5th patch would risk repeating the exact pattern just diagnosed. This session honored that recommendation. `sanctuary-ui.md`'s status header, the review log, and `systems-index.md` all reflect this honestly — the document is `Needs Revision`, explicitly frozen, not silently left looking more finished than it is.
- Flagged the knock-on effect: `tarot-deck-ui.md`'s own `Approved` status now carries a caveat, since its entry mechanism (Core Rule 3) depends directly on Sanctuary UI's Core Rule 10, which is exactly what's being frozen for redesign. Tarot Deck UI's own Core Rules 1–7 are not implicated and remain sound — only the entry-point dependency needs re-checking once Core Rule 10 is redone.
- **Lesson for whoever picks up Core Rule 10 next**: don't quick-patch it. Design the Loadout affordance's focus model, terminal-latch integration, and narrative placement deliberately, together, in one pass — the failure mode observed tonight was treating it as a series of independent small fixes instead of one coherent addition.

8. The 2 remaining `Partial` items (Epic 06 Stories 012/013) need a real human playtest pass — cannot be closed autonomously.
9. ~~Remaining "Not Started" GDDs: Encyclopedia, Audio System~~ — **both done this session, both Approved**. No "Not Started" GDDs remain anywhere in the project.

### Sanctuary UI Core Rule 10: deliberately redesigned (5th pass), not just patched — plus 2 sibling-doc sync fixes
- Per the 4th pass's own explicit freeze directive, came back to this with a genuine redesign rather than another quick patch. **The fix**: Loadout (the hand-off to Tarot Deck UI) is no longer a bolted-on `OVERVIEW`-only content-zone button sharing an ambiguous focus mechanism with Launch/Depart — it is now the tab strip's own **6th entry** (Core Rule 2), selectable from any state exactly like the other 5, with one behavioral difference (it triggers a shell exit instead of a room mount). This single structural change dissolved 5 of the 4th pass's 6 named blocking issues by construction, not by patching around them: no new `InputModeTracker`, no "trailing entries" retrofit, no terminal-latch race against a shared mechanism (Launch/Depart and Loadout are now genuinely separate widgets), no tab-strip-entry-count self-contradiction, and Loadout is now integrated into the Player Fantasy section with real prose (previously it appeared nowhere in Player Fantasy/Overview at all).
- **Also fixed**: Loadout is now reachable from any Sanctuary UI state, not `OVERVIEW`-only (a deliberate, explained departure from Launch/Depart's own OVERVIEW-only scope, since Loadout is a lateral navigation, not a session-ending action) — a genuine UX improvement, not just a bug fix.
- **Independent verification** (a fresh-context `systems-designer` pass) confirmed the redesign structurally sound but found 2 real gaps this session's own momentum had missed: (1) two sibling documents — `tarot-deck-ui.md` and `design/ux/sanctuary-ui.md` — still described the pre-redesign OVERVIEW-only mechanism, meaning the redesign had gone out of sync with its own dependents; both fixed same-session (`tarot-deck-ui.md`'s 3 references corrected fully; the UX spec's core zone-rules table corrected, with its still-stale wireframes/binding-table honestly flagged as a new Open Question rather than silently redrawn without a full pass or silently left wrong). (2) The redesign's own terminal-latch rationale overstated its safety claim ("genuinely different physical inputs" — false; both buttons share the same confirm input, only the focus *path* differs) — corrected to describe the real, narrower risk. Also added a previously-unenumerated Edge Case + AC pair (staged Equipment Forge/Defense Building state discarded when selecting Loadout mid-room, not just on ordinary tab switches).
- **Deliberately NOT marked Approved.** This document has now been substantively edited across 5 passes spanning 2 separate work sessions tonight. `systems-index.md` and the GDD's own status header both explicitly recommend a genuinely fresh, full 6-specialist `/design-review` as the next step — not another same-session verification pass — before Approved status, matching this project's own standing precedent (`mission-hud.md` required an actual review run, not just self-checks, before its Approved flip).

### Sanctuary UI: 6th pass — fresh full `/design-review` run, converged, Approved
- Ran the recommended fresh full 6-specialist review on the redesigned document. Found one convergent defect (independently spotted from 4 different angles — a hand-off ordering/terminal-latch contradiction, a room-teardown signal-leak gap where a room's own EventBus subscriptions weren't covered by the shell-level disconnect, and an unstated animation-ownership risk) plus smaller findings (the Launch/Depart-vs-Loadout reachability asymmetry had no stated rationale; the sibling UX spec's layout question was tracked as stale but never actually answered; the hand-off mechanism was less precise than its own stated model; a new Player Fantasy claim had zero test coverage). All fixed same-session — see `design/gdd/reviews/sanctuary-ui-review-log.md` for full detail.
- An independent re-verification pass found 2 narrow residual issues (a wording precision in the terminal-latch rule that technically still contradicted the redesign's own step ordering; one AC that only tested half of a two-part teardown fix) — both closed immediately.
- **Sanctuary UI is now `Approved`.** This closes the loop that started with the 4th-pass freeze earlier tonight: found broken → frozen deliberately rather than patched → redesigned properly → reviewed fresh → converged to clean. `tarot-deck-ui.md`'s own caveat (its entry point depended on this document) is now fully resolved — both documents are Approved with no outstanding cross-document tension.
- **All 3 UI-shell/meta-progression GDDs touched tonight are now Approved**: Sanctuary UI, Tarot Deck UI, and (from earlier) Encyclopedia and Audio System are also Approved — 11 of 25 systems in this project now carry Approved status, the highest this project has ever had.

### Encyclopedia System: authored from scratch, reviewed, Approved (2026-07-24)
- User explicitly asked to keep going with Encyclopedia and Audio GDDs after the Sanctuary UI freeze summary. Authored `design/gdd/encyclopedia.md` fully from scratch (previously 100% unstarted) — a passive, cross-cutting discovery log subscribing to 7 already-frozen EventBus events across 7 upstream systems (Constellation, Tarot, Crafting, Equipment Forge, Menagerie, Defense Building, Tower Mastery/Hero Skill Build), converting each into a permanent categorized entry, emitting one outbound event (`encyclopedia_entry_added`) for Mission HUD's own toast.
- **Player Fantasy** ("The Game's Memory of You") deliberately frames the system as never owning a discovery "moment" — every other system's own reveal already has its own spotlight; Encyclopedia is pure testimony, filed away afterward. Anchored to Pillar 3 (Sanctuary Is Your Statement) as the sanctuary's textual twin; explicitly declines Pillar 1 entirely.
- **Real design decisions made and documented**: explicitly declined the "Encyclopedia as Tarot-card-purchase-mechanism" candidate `tarot.md` had left open (architectural reason: would create a second uncoordinated writer on Tarot's `unlocked` flag alongside Defense Building's tech tree); deferred a monster/bestiary codex entirely (no one-shot "first encounter" event exists yet for any monster species — flagged for `monster.md`); deferred completion-percentage tracking (no GDD anywhere authors a stable total-count denominator per category); and — most consequentially — **deliberately deferred designing any UI/screen for itself**, citing this same session's own costly experience bolting a late entry point onto `sanctuary-ui.md`. Considered and explicitly rejected asserting Encyclopedia's place inside either existing UI shell unilaterally.
- **Full `/design-review` found NEEDS REVISION on first pass**: a real self-contradiction (an AC required storing `recipe_category` on an entry; the core data-model rule said entries are exactly 3 fields, no more); an unfalsifiable Player Fantasy (no query contract existed anywhere for any consumer — including a future UI — to actually read the ledger); a mis-scoped AC testing a cross-system race claim Encyclopedia's own code can't actually prove; an unearned "NEW flair" implication with no supporting unread-flag field; and a real, adjudicated design tension (does species-level creature-discovery tracking undercut the "specific player" Player Fantasy, given Menagerie already tracks individual creature identity?).
- **Fixed same-session**: added a minimal read-only query contract (`get_entries()`/`is_discovered()`) — this single addition closed the "unfalsifiable fantasy" finding without touching a screen, and creative-director's synthesis explicitly credited it as the right-sized fix. Made `recipe_category` a render-time lookup instead of a stored field, resolving the contradiction. Split the mis-scoped AC into a narrow, Encyclopedia-owned Logic AC and a separately-owned Integration AC. Added a "Data Requirements for a Future UI" note (what sort/group operations the data model DOES support today, and the one it explicitly doesn't — unread tracking) without designing any actual screen. Adjudicated the granularity tension: species-level confirmed correct (Encyclopedia witnesses, Menagerie owns individual identity — `menagerie.md`'s own text already agrees in 3 places).
- **Independent fresh-context re-verification**: confirmed all 5 problems structurally closed, and — notably — explicitly endorsed the UI-deferral decision as "the single most disciplined decision in the document," not under-design, directly validating the lesson learned earlier this session with Sanctuary UI. Found and fixed one real remaining gap: an Open Question (no confirmed display-content lookup path exists for 2 of the 6 entry categories) was under-weighted relative to how much it actually limits the Player Fantasy's eventual delivery — elevated its priority explicitly.
- **This document is now `Approved`** in `systems-index.md` — the 3rd GDD this session to reach that status (alongside Hero Skill Build, pre-existing, and Tarot Deck UI, earlier tonight). All Full-Vision-tier systems now have exactly one remaining "Not Started" GDD: Audio System.
- Notably: this authoring cycle did NOT reproduce the Sanctuary UI fatigue pattern, despite going through the identical full-review → fix → independent-re-verify cycle — the difference was authoring the whole document in one clean pass with specialist consultation from the start, rather than accumulating patches across multiple separate sessions/passes on an already-shipped document. Worth remembering as the actual lesson: it's *incremental patching of an already-reviewed document*, not thoroughness itself, that produced tonight's earlier fatigue cascade.

### Audio System GDD: authored from scratch, reviewed, Approved (2026-07-24) — the last "Not Started" GDD in the project
- Authored `design/gdd/audio-system.md` fully from scratch (previously 100% unstarted, despite being named as a Key Subscriber on 16 already-frozen EventBus events spanning nearly the entire game). Consulted `audio-director` for mixing philosophy/direction, `systems-designer` for technical architecture, and `qa-lead` for ACs during authoring, matching the Encyclopedia precedent (clean one-pass authoring with specialist consultation from the start, not incremental patching).
- **Architecture**: 4 player-facing buses (Music/SFX/Voice/UI) — this exact list is a binding, pre-existing commitment from `design/accessibility-requirements.md`'s Basic tier, not invented here. State-driven music/ambient model mapped 1:1 to `art-bible.md` Section 2's own 4 Energy levels (Contemplative/Measured/Urgent/Measured-urgent), with a specific anticipatory crossfade for SCOUT→WAVE_ACTIVE and a deliberate hard-cut exception for WAVE_ACTIVE→INTERMISSION's "held breath released" beat. A 7-family SFX cue taxonomy gives ~10 other GDDs' own previously-scattered, ad-hoc "audio direction, no bible yet" notes a shared vocabulary to sort into. Data-driven `AudioCueMap` registration means most future systems' own audio needs require zero code changes to Audio System, only data.
- **Real cross-system precedent established**: volume/mute settings persist outside Save & Persistence's `restore_order` system entirely (their own `ConfigFile`, since they're a device/session preference, not per-save-slot progress) — flagged explicitly as a pattern future preference-style systems (input remapping, named as the next obvious candidate) should follow rather than rediscovering.
- **Full `/design-review` found NEEDS REVISION on first pass**: one blocking untestable AC (asserted an absence of a code path, not a runtime-observable claim); most significantly, a Player Fantasy overreach identical in shape to earlier findings tonight — claimed "genuinely both primary" pillars (Everything Connects + The Sanctuary Is Your Statement) when the second had zero implementing mechanic anywhere (no continuous sanctuary-investment signal exists on the EventBus, no formula, no rule); an oversold architectural claim; a real Godot 4.6 data-structure implementability risk (`Dictionary[StringName, Resource]` in a `.tres` has no usable inspector); an ambiguous bus-routing description; an unhandled music-crossfade drift case; 4 of 6 cue families with unspecified voice caps despite the rule claiming universal coverage; 2 compound ACs; and a "closes an existing gap" claim not yet backed by an edit to the referenced document.
- **Fixed same-session**: split/rewrote the untestable AC; narrowed the Player Fantasy to Everything Connects-primary only (demoting the Sanctuary claim to a tracked Open Question, not silently dropping it — the exact correction pattern already applied to `tarot-deck-ui.md`'s own Player Fantasy earlier tonight); precisely reworded the architectural claim; switched `AudioCueMap` from Dictionary to Array per the real Godot inspector limitation; clarified the bus-routing model explicitly; added a resync fallback for the anticipatory crossfade; added placeholder voice caps for all 6 families (explicitly flagged as unvalidated); split both compound ACs; softened the cross-doc claim (`event-bus.md` itself still needs its own one-line edit, flagged as that document's own gap to close, matching the precedent Encyclopedia's review established for an analogous finding).
- **Independent fresh-context re-verification**: confirmed all 9 problems structurally closed — notably verified `event-bus.md`'s stale text directly rather than trusting the claim, and confirmed the new placeholder voice caps are honestly flagged as unvalidated rather than presented with false confidence. Found and fixed 3 small residual issues (a broken AC cross-reference, a duplicated edge case, one subtly-overstated claim in the revised Player Fantasy).
- **This document is now `Approved`** — the 4th GDD this session to reach that status. **Every system in this project now has a GDD, and both Full Vision-tier systems (Encyclopedia, Audio System) are now designed and approved.** The systems-index Design Docs Started count is 25/25 for the first time in this project's history.

### ADR-0032: Presentation Shell Hosting Pattern — authored, Accepted (2026-07-24)
- Both Epic 10 (Sanctuary UI) and Epic 11 (Tarot Deck UI) independently flagged the same real gap: no dedicated architecture ADR existed for the destroy-and-recreate + terminal-latch mechanism their own GDDs' multi-pass review histories had converged on. Rather than write two near-duplicate ADRs, wrote one shared ADR covering both shells — matching Epic 11's own explicit recommendation when it was created earlier this session.
- **Architecture registry checked first** (`docs/registry/architecture.yaml`) — no existing stance conflicted; the closest related entries (`ui_input_mode_tracking` from ADR-0011, `scene_transition` from ADR-0002) were confirmed as dependencies, not contradictions.
- **godot-specialist validation spawned and returned before writing**: confirmed the pattern (disconnect + input-gate synchronously BEFORE `queue_free()`, shared `_departing` flag with disconnect-not-just-flag-check) is correct Godot 4.6.2 usage, matches `mission_hud.gd`'s own already-shipped `_terminal`/Planning Cluster precedent exactly, and that no cleaner idiomatic alternative exists (`Node.set_block_signals(true)` considered and rejected as too blunt an instrument). Flagged 2 real risks now captured in the ADR: hand-maintained disconnect lists can silently drift, and `InputModeTracker` roster teardown/construction ordering must not interleave.
- Status **Accepted** directly (review-mode=lean skips the TD-ADR technical-director gate per the skill's own Step 4.6 rule) — not left as `Proposed`, since both epics' stories were already written and marked `Ready` (not `Blocked`) against this exact pattern in prose; this ADR formalizes what was already implementable, it doesn't newly unblock anything.
- **Registry updated**: new interface contract `presentation_shell_hosting` (referencing both ADR-0032 and its ADR-0031 precedent) and new forbidden pattern `keep_alive_hidden_shell_content` (the explicitly-rejected Alternative 1 — keeping rooms/screens alive-but-hidden instead of destroy-and-recreate).
- **Both epics' EPIC.md files updated**: the "real gap, flagged not hidden" notes in Epic 10 and Epic 11 now read "Gap closed 2026-07-24" with a pointer to ADR-0032. Sanctuary UI Story 007/010 and Tarot Deck UI Story 003 (the 3 stories whose Governing Implementation most directly depended on this missing ADR) had their own Context sections updated to cite ADR-0032 as the direct source, while preserving the still-open ADR-0020 Criterion A/B classification flags (OQ-2/OQ-9) as separate, unresolved items this ADR does not cover.
- Per the skill's own standing rule, this ADR was authored and Accepted in the SAME session as story-writing work on the epics it supports — **`/architecture-review` must be run in a fresh session**, never in this one, to independently validate cross-ADR coverage without authoring-context bias. Flagged as the next architecture-track task, not run tonight.
- **Both Presentation-layer epics (10, 11) are now in the strongest state they've been in this project**: GDDs Approved, epics written with ADR coverage complete for the mechanics that matter most, 19 stories total all `Ready` with zero `Blocked`. The only remaining structural gaps are the pre-existing, deliberately-still-open ones (zero TR-IDs registered for either system, ADR-0020 classification pending for the hand-off calls) — both already flagged repeatedly, not new discoveries.
- **Next priority items, updated**: (1) `/architecture-review` in a fresh session, to independently validate ADR-0032 and the whole project's cross-ADR state now that all 25 systems have GDDs and 11 epics exist; (2) `/ux-design` follow-up on `design/ux/sanctuary-ui.md` and new `/ux-design tarot-deck-ui`; (3) the 2 remaining Epic 06 `Partial` stories need a real human playtest; (4) Audio System's `AudioCueMap` content-authoring; (5) actual implementation (`/story-readiness` → `/dev-story`) can now begin on either Epic 10 or Epic 11 — Story 001 in each is the natural starting point.

## Session Extract — /code-review remediation (Sanctuary UI) 2026-07-24
- Fixed all 4 Required Changes from the Sanctuary UI code review (spawned in parallel with a Tarot Deck UI review this session):
  1. `room_host.gd`: drawer-slide tween now stored in `_transition_tween`, `kill_transition_tween()` added, called automatically at the start of every new slide-in — closes the rapid-re-trigger orphaned-tween risk (GDD's own named Edge Case).
  2. `room_host.gd`: removed the decoy `InputModeTracker` injection from `mount_room()` — each room now owns its own tracker (matches `InventoryScreen.tscn`'s real `$InputModeTracker`), `get_current_room_tracker()` changed to a `get_node_or_null()` lookup by conventional child name.
  3. `sanctuary_ui.gd`: `set_active_state()` now keeps the diorama visible during `DEPARTING_STATE` (previously fell into the generic "room active" branch and incorrectly hid it, contradicting the GDD's camera-pull-back requirement).
  4. `sanctuary_ui.gd`: `select_loadout()` Step 1 now actually calls `_room_host.kill_transition_tween()` (was a stale placeholder comment); added missing doc comment to `get_active_state()`.
- Fixed a regression the tracker-decoy removal (#2) would have introduced: `tests/integration/sanctuary-ui/room_lifecycle_stub_room.gd`'s stub had no `InputModeTracker` child, so the 2 tracker-identity tests in `room-lifecycle_test.gd` would have started comparing `null == null`. Added a real `InputModeTracker` child node to `RoomLifecycleStubRoom.tscn` (matching the `InventoryScreen.tscn` convention) — no test code needed to change, since the tests were already asserting the correct behavior, just against a previously-decoy object.
- Similarly fixed the Tarot Deck UI review's 1 real finding: `tarot_deck_ui.gd`'s `confirm_return_to_sanctuary()` had no ADR-0032 Section 2 terminal latch — added `_departing` flag, disconnect of `_tab_strip.tab_target_selected` and a new `ConflictIndicator.disconnect_own_subscriptions()` (idempotency-guarded against double-disconnect from `_exit_tree()`).
- Rebuilt the Godot class cache (`--headless --editor --path . --quit`) and ran the full `tests/integration/sanctuary-ui/` + `tests/integration/tarot-deck-ui/` suites: **108/108 passing, 0 errors, 0 failures, 0 orphans**. Full project-wide regression also launched to confirm no wider breakage.
- Next: run `/story-done` on every Complete story in Epic 10 (Sanctuary UI, 001-012) and Epic 11 (Tarot Deck UI, 001-005), per the user's original "run /code-review then /story-done on everything complete" request. Story 013 (Partial) and Story 014 (Ready, not attempted) are not story-done-able as Complete.

Added it as a shell-owned child of `SanctuaryUI.tscn` (persists across room switches within one Sanctuary visit, resets on scene reload, matching ADR-0025's own design) plus a new `"equipment_forge"` Godot group so the RoomHost-hosted screen can discover it without RoomHost's generic mount mechanism needing a room-specific injection seam. - Tarot is also scene-instanced (ADR-0027) — added as a shell-owned child of `TarotDeckUI.tscn` via the same group pattern (`"tarot"` group). Hero Skill Build is a real Autoload (`GHeroSkillBuild`), needed no such wiring.
- **Real gap closed as a direct consequence**: `SnapshotChip.get_tarot_risk_summary()` previously hardcoded `{"available": false}` since no live Tarot instance existed anywhere. Now discovers the shell-owned instance via the group and returns a real `equipped_card_count` reading — this was Story 005's own last-remaining deferred AC, now fully closed (4/4 → still 4/4 criteria but genuinely all-real; test count 4→6).

## Session Extract — Cross-shell hand-off wiring 2026-07-25 (continued autonomous session)
- Per user's "continue with the cross-shell hand-offs" instruction.
- **Loadout → Tarot Deck UI: fully wired, real, tested.** Added `SceneManager.transition_to_tarot_deck_ui()` (mirrors `transition_to_sanctuary()` exactly) + a new `TarotDeckUIScene.tscn` top-level scene wrapping the real `TarotDeckUI.tscn` shell (mirrors `sanctuary_scene.gd`'s own identical wrapper pattern). `sanctuary_ui.gd`'s `select_loadout()` Step 4 now calls it for real — the previously-flagged "no scene-transition entry point exists" gap is closed. This is a genuine expansion beyond ADR-0002's current Decision text (2 contexts: SANCTUARY/MISSION) to a 3rd lateral context (TAROT_DECK) — flagged explicitly in the ADR's own Ordering Note and sanctuary-ui.md's OQ-9, not silently treated as already covered; ADR-0020's Criterion A/B classification remains formally open. `loadout-handoff_test.gd` updated to exercise the real call safely via `SceneManager`'s own established `_inject()` test seam (matching `tarot-deck-ui/exit-and-lifecycle_test.gd`'s identical pattern), plus a new test asserting the real `scene_transition_started("TAROT_DECK")` emission. Committed and pushed (`3e39c28`).
- **Launch/Depart → Mission Select: investigated, found to be a substantially bigger gap than initially scoped, deliberately NOT attempted this pass.** Real finding: this isn't just "no Mission Select browsing screen" (which would be a comparable scope to tonight's other 6 screens) — `MissionSelect.confirm_launch()` itself doesn't call `SceneManager.transition_to_mission()` at all; nothing in the entire project currently bridges "Mission Select confirms a launch" to an actual SANCTUARY→MISSION scene transition. `MissionScene.tscn` (the generic mission-loading scene `SceneManager.transition_to_mission()` targets) has never existed — the only real, playable mission scene is `the_gap.tscn`, a directly-boot, single hardcoded mission assembled by Epic 04 Story 008, structurally unrelated to `SceneManager`'s transition flow. Building this hand-off for real would require: (1) a generic `MissionScene.tscn` that can load an arbitrary mission by ID from `MissionRuntimeState` (initializing Grid/WaveManager/Monster/Tower/Hero generically, the way `the_gap.tscn` does for one hardcoded case), (2) wiring `confirm_launch()` (or a subscriber) to actually trigger `SceneManager.transition_to_mission()`, AND (3) building the actual Mission Select browsing screen itself (mission list + difficulty/modifier panel + persistent launch bar with its own two-step arm/confirm pattern, per `mission-select.md`'s real UI Requirements section, which does exist and is well-specified). This is a foundational-architecture-scale initiative — comparable to or larger than Epic 04 Story 008's own original "assemble already-built subsystems into one real scene" work — not a same-class "wire an existing shell" task like Loadout→Tarot Deck UI was. Deliberately not rushed under a single pass's time pressure; flagged clearly rather than shipping something shaky. `sanctuary_ui.gd`'s own `confirm_launch_depart()` still calls the same flagged placeholder (`GMissionSelect.back_out_to_browsing()`) it did before — genuinely unchanged, not silently worked around.
- **Recommended next pickup** (a new, separate initiative, not a quick follow-on): scope and build the generic mission-loading scene + Mission Select browsing screen as its own dedicated pass, likely starting with a `/design-system` or direct UI-Requirements-driven build session mirroring tonight's pattern, given `mission-select.md`'s UI Requirements section is already well-specified.

- **2 real bugs found and fixed in `MissionSelect`** while wiring this: (1) `confirm_launch()` never called `Grid.initialize()` on first launch (only `retry_current_mission()` did) — WaveManager reads Grid synchronously on `mission_launched`, so first launches were reading stale/empty Grid state; (2) `reset_to_defaults()` only cleared `_completion_ledger`, leaving `state`/`_current_mission_id`/`difficulty_tier`/`_run_modifiers`/every `_runtime_*` field untouched — found via cross-test leakage in the new screen's own test suite.

- **Test-authoring bug found and fixed** (not a production bug): `mission_scene_test.gd`'s first 3 tests originally did `GMissionSelect.confirm_launch(); await GEventBus.mission_launched` — but `mission_launched` is emitted *synchronously* inside `confirm_launch()` and `MissionScene` connects to it directly (not `CONNECT_DEFERRED`), so the signal had already fired by the time the `await` line ran, hanging every one of those 3 tests for the full 5-minute test timeout in the first background run. Fixed by removing the unneeded `await` — the content swap and Grid init are already complete by the time `confirm_launch()` returns. Re-ran in isolation: 4/4 passing, 0 errors, 1.2s total (down from 15min timeout territory).
- Documentation fully updated to reflect the resolution: `sanctuary-ui.md` OQ-2, `story-009-exit-to-mission-select.md` (status header, 2 ACs, Out of Scope gap paragraph, Completion Notes), `EPIC.md`'s Story 009 row and closure-status note, and `adr-0002-scene-autoload-structure.md`'s Ordering Note (documents that MISSION did NOT gain a 4th top-level context the way TAROT_DECK did — one scene now hosts both browsing and gameplay internally).
- **Full project regression run** (1662 test cases, 173 suites, 23min): 3 errors (the since-fixed `mission_scene_test.gd` await-hangs — confirmed exactly accounted for, nothing else), 1 failure (`tests/unit/menagerie/lure_acquisition_test.gd:77`, `test_lure_creature_success_full_flow` — expected '0' got '1'; **unrelated to any file touched this session**, not investigated further this pass, flagged here as a new open item), 2258 orphans (TD-test-004's known pre-existing orphan-count anomaly, still not root-caused — grew further, worth prioritizing its bisection soon).

## Session Extract — Code review, TD-menag-006, and TD-test-004 bisection 2026-07-25 (continued autonomous session)
- Per user's "perform code-review this work, investigate the menagerie test failure, and the overdue orphan-count bisection" instruction — all 3 items from the previous extract's recommended-next-pickup list, done in one pass.
- **`/code-review` on the Mission Select work**: spawned `godot-gdscript-specialist` for an independent pass over `mission_select.gd`, `mission_select_screen.gd`, `mission_scene.gd`, `game_data_registry.gd`, `sanctuary_ui.gd`. One real WARNING-level finding, fixed immediately: `mission_select_screen.gd` connected 2 EventBus subscriptions in `_ready()` with no `_exit_tree()` to disconnect them, violating the control manifest's zero-exception EventBus rule — added a `_departing`-guard-style idempotent `_exit_tree()` that calls the existing `disconnect_own_subscriptions()`. 3 further non-blocking findings logged (not fixed, judged genuinely non-blocking): `CELL_SIZE=64.0` duplicated verbatim across `mission_scene.gd`/`the_gap.gd` with no shared source of truth; `MonsterManager._entity_lookup` private-field cross-class access now propagated into a 2nd file; no `focus_neighbor_*` wiring on `mission_select_screen.gd`'s button groups despite ADR-0011 requiring it for custom adjacency (gamepad is optional/partial for this project, so not blocking). 16/16 affected tests re-confirmed passing after the `_exit_tree()` fix.
- **TD-menag-006 (menagerie test failure) — root-caused and fixed.** The TEST's expectation was wrong, not the code: `menagerie.md`'s own AC-4 states a Lure with an open HABITAT slot places the creature directly into HABITAT, not RESERVE — `menagerie.gd`'s `lure_creature()` already implemented this correctly; `lure_acquisition_test.gd:77` asserted `CreatureState.RESERVE` for a fresh Menagerie's first lure (habitat_base_capacity=4, so HABITAT is open), contradicting its own GDD. Fixed the test assertion to `HABITAT`, updated its comment to cite AC-4. Re-verified 6/6 passing. Tech-debt register entry closed.
- **TD-test-004 (orphan-count regression) — bisected via a new methodology (subdivide-and-isolate, not the originally-planned SanctuaryScene.tscn revert), 2 of at least 3 contributors found and fixed.** Reproduced the full signature at small scale (`tests/integration/sanctuary-ui/` alone: 955 orphans / 86 tests, vs. 2258/1662 full-suite), then bisected suite-by-suite within that directory to isolate exactly 2 files: `exit-mission-select_test.gd` (679 orphans/8 tests) and `tab-strip-navigation_test.gd` (178 orphans/17 tests). Root cause: both files call real `SanctuaryUI` methods (`confirm_launch_depart()`, tab-6/Loadout navigation) that trigger genuine `GSceneManager.transition_to_mission("")`/`transition_to_tarot_deck_ui()` calls, but neither test file used `SceneManager`'s own established `_inject()` stub-scene test seam (`loadout-handoff_test.gd` already does this correctly) — before this session those transition targets either didn't exist or weren't wired, so the calls were inert; as `MissionScene.tscn` and `TarotDeckUIScene.tscn` became real and heavy over the course of this session, these 2 already-existing tests silently became the dominant orphan source (real, node-heavy scene trees loaded and never torn down, since `scene_runner()`'s auto-free only manages its own instantiated `SanctuaryUI`). Fixed by adding the identical `_inject()`/restore-in-`after_test()` seam to both files. Isolated verification: `sanctuary-ui` directory 955→48 orphans (86/86 still passing, ~95% reduction). **Full 173-suite regression only dropped 2258→1343** (0 errors/0 failures both before and after — never a correctness issue) — confirms these were a genuine major contributor but not the only one; the residual ~1343 (vs. ~158 original baseline) means at least one more contributor remains unidentified elsewhere in the 173 suites. Tech-debt entry updated honestly as partially-fixed/reopened, not falsely closed.
- Full regression evidence this pass: 1662 tests, 0 errors, 0 failures (both the `mission_scene_test.gd` await-hangs and `lure_acquisition_test.gd`'s failure from the previous extract are now gone), 1343 orphans (down from 2258).
- All 4 touched files committed together in one commit (mission_select_screen.gd's `_exit_tree()` fix, lure_acquisition_test.gd's assertion fix, exit-mission-select_test.gd's and tab-strip-navigation_test.gd's stub-scene seams) plus the tech-debt register updates.
- **Recommended next pickup**: (1) finish TD-test-004's remaining ~1343-orphan residual — repeat this exact subdivide-and-isolate methodology across the other ~160 suites not yet checked; (2) Story 014 (Sanctuary UI's own remaining Experiential Playtest story) and Story 013's diorama-blocked remainder; (3) the 4 non-blocking code-review findings logged above, if/when their files are next touched.

- **Implemented all 3 stories in one pass** (Encyclopedia's GDD is much smaller than Audio's — 16 ACs across 5 groups vs. Audio's 15+ across 6 — so all 3 stories shared one class, `src/core/encyclopedia/encyclopedia.gd`, built incrementally):
  - **Story 001 (Discovery Event Ingestion)**: subscribes to 7 already-frozen EventBus discovery events (skill_unlocked, constellation_rune/tarot_rune_discovered, recipe_discovered, creature_acquired/bred, tech_unlocked), keyed by `(category, entry_id)`. Creature discovery is species-level per Core Rule 8 (keyed by `species`, never the per-instance `creature_id`). 11/11 tests.

AC-15b (upstream systems don't refire during their own restore) tested against real `Constellation`/`Tarot` instances. 10/10 tests (7 original + 3 code-review regression tests).

  - Also caught 2 of my own bugs during test-writing, before code review: `discovered_at` was stored as a float (`Time.get_unix_time_from_system()`'s real return type) when the GDD specifies `int` — fixed with an explicit cast; a unit test's directly-instantiated `Encyclopedia` `Node` was never freed, causing 7 orphans — fixed by adding `.free()` to `after_test()`.
- **`/code-review`** (spawned `godot-gdscript-specialist`) found and fixed a real gap: `restore_save_data()` had no `is Array`/`is Dictionary`/per-field type guards before its typed loop — a malformed save would throw a runtime type error instead of returning `false` gracefully. Fixed to match Menagerie's own established defense-in-depth pattern exactly (the identical bug class already found and fixed there earlier this session, per that file's own review-finding comment). Added 3 regression tests. Also fixed 1 INFO nit (`get_entries()` returning untyped `Array` instead of `Array[Dictionary]`).
- 28 gdUnit4 tests across the epic, all passing, 0 orphans. Full project regression run 3 times this session (after implementation, after code-review fixes) — all clean, orphan count held steady at 1459 throughout (TD-test-004's pre-existing baseline, untouched this session).
- Formally closed all 3 stories with `## Completion Notes` (matching Epic 12's own `/story-done` bookkeeping pattern) in the same pass as implementation, rather than as a separate follow-up step.

## Session Extract — TD-test-004 substantially resolved, 2026-07-25 (continued autonomous session)
- User said "continue development" — picked up TD-test-004 (item #1 from the prior extract's recommended-next-pickup list), the orphan-node regression that had been bisected across 3 prior passes this session down to "an interaction effect requiring specific Autoload-subscriber combinations, order-dependent, not fully root-caused" and deliberately deferred each time as low value-per-effort to chase further.
- **Found a practical workaround without needing the full root cause**: re-read the 3 confirmed contributor files (`mission_economy_test.gd`, `tower_combat_test.gd`, `tower_placement_test.gd`) and noticed all 3 used `GEventBus.mission_launched.emit()` purely as `before_test()`/`after_test()` boilerplate to reset `GMissionEconomy`'s gold balance — none were actually testing the broadcast mechanism itself. Hypothesis: broadcasting cascades into `WaveManager`'s own handler too (the only one of `mission_launched`'s 5 subscribers that manages real Node-based state — phase timers, spawn scheduling, per `wave_manager.gd:748`), and repeating that cascade across many gdUnit4 test-case boundaries is what leaks.
- **Tested the hypothesis directly**: replaced the 3 files' boilerplate reset calls with a direct call to `GMissionEconomy._on_mission_launched(...)` (the exact same reset logic, bypassing the broadcast to the other 4 Autoloads). The 2 tests in `mission_economy_test.gd` that specifically verify AC-11's broadcast behavior were left unchanged (still use the real `.emit()`). Verified in isolation before applying broadly: each file individually dropped to 0 orphans, all tests still passing.
- **Full project regression: 1459 → 185 orphans (an 87% reduction), 0 errors/0 failures both before and after** — confirms the hypothesis was correct without needing to fully resolve the earlier-noted "combination/order-dependent interaction effect." This is easily the largest single reduction TD-test-004 has seen across its 4 bisection/fix passes this session (previous best was ~40% from the Sanctuary UI `_inject()` seam fix).
- Updated `docs/tech-debt-register.md`'s TD-test-004 entry to reflect substantial resolution — closed the checklist item, left the residual 185 (scattered, not isolated to one dominant contributor) as an explicitly lower-priority follow-up rather than continuing to chase it this pass.
- Committed and pushed (`eebd43b`): the 3 test file fixes + tech-debt register update.
- **Recommended next pickup**: (1) Epic 10's Story 013 (diorama art, blocked on local GPU/ZLUDA driver issue) / Story 014 (needs a real human playtester) — both remain the only genuinely externally-blocked items in the whole project; (2) the residual 185 orphans, if ever worth a dedicated pass; (3) the small honestly-flagged, non-blocking gaps from this session's code reviews (Audio System's unwired sidechain ducking, Story 004's unwired VoiceLimiter dispatch) — worth revisiting once real content authoring begins for those systems. With every system now having an epic and TD-test-004 substantially resolved, the project is in a genuinely clean state — there is no large, obviously-next autonomous unit of work remaining; future sessions should likely take direction from the user on priority (new content authoring, a fresh system design pass, or infrastructure/tooling work) rather than another self-selected epic.

## Session Extract — VoiceLimiter wired into live dispatch, 2026-07-25 (continued autonomous session)
- User said "continue" — attempted to pick up `tarot_wave_config_override`'s own gap next (the item flagged in the prior extract), but investigating it first revealed it's not content-authoring at all: `WaveManager` has zero code handling this modifier type, and `TarotModifierEntry.base_modifier_value` is a plain `float` that structurally cannot hold a reference to an alternate `WaveConfig` resource — closing it would mean designing a new resolution mechanism (likely a schema change) and writing new engine code, not authoring data. - Picked a different, already-well-scoped follow-up instead: wired `VoiceLimiter` (Story 004) into `AudioCueDispatcher`'s real `dispatch_cue()` path (Story 003) — a deliberate, already-fully-designed-and-tested deferral from earlier this session's own code review, not a new design decision. `dispatch_cue()` now calls `voice_limiter.trigger_voice(entry.polyphony_group)` on every real dispatch. Added 3 new tests proving the wiring through a real `GEventBus`-driven dispatch (not just a direct `VoiceLimiter` call): voice registration, oldest-voice-stealing beyond Discovery Fanfare's real cap of 1, and no-registration for cues with an empty `polyphony_group`.
- Explicitly left `VoiceLimiter.compute_ducked_volume()` (Formula F1) unwired — no dispatch-time concept of "how many voices are concurrently active" exists yet to feed it; a separate, still-open follow-up, not conflated with the voice-cap wiring just closed.

- **Recommended next pickup**: same externally-blocked/lower-priority items as every recent extract (Epic 10 Story 013/014, residual 185 orphans, Audio System's unwired sidechain ducking, `tarot_wave_config_override`'s now-scoped-and-declined implementation gap).
